\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}\Big[f(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n})\mathbbm{I}\big\{\bar{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}\notin\bar{\mathbb{A}}(\bm{k})\big\}\Big]}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}=0;

2.

there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that
for all but countably many
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}\Big[f(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n})\mathbbm{I}\big\{\bar{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}=(\bm{\mathcal{K}},0)\big\}\Big]}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}=\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(f)<\infty,\qquad\forall\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k}),

where the measure
\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(\cdot)=\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{{}_{[0,1]}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{0}}(\cdot)
is defined in (
3.10
).

We defer their proofs to Section
D.2
,
and conclude this subsection by applying Propositions
D.2
and
D.3
and establishing Theorem
3.2
.

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

Without loss of generality, we prove Theorem
3.2
under Assumption
6
.
For any
\epsilon>0
and any Borel set
B\subseteq\mathbb{D}
that is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}
,
note that
B^{\Delta}
is also bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}
under any
\Delta>0
sufficiently small.
By Urysohn’s Lemma, one can identify some bounded and continuous
f:\mathbb{D}\to[0,1]
such that
\mathbbm{I}_{B}\leq f\leq\mathbbm{I}_{B^{\Delta}}
.
Then, part (b) of Proposition
D.3
confirms that

\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(B)\leq\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(f)<\infty
.
This establishes the finite upper bound in Claim (
3.19
) and verifies that
\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\in\mathbb{M}\big(\mathbb{D}\setminus\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}\big)
, thus allowing us to apply Lemma
A.3
.
Next, given
\epsilon>0
and
under the choice of

\displaystyle(\mathbb{S},\bm{d})=(\mathbb{D},\bm{d}_{J_{1}}),\quad\mathbb{C}=\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}},\quad\epsilon_{n}=\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n),\quad X_{n}=\bar{\bm{L}}_{n},\quad Y^{\delta}_{n}=\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n},\quad V^{\delta}_{n}=\bar{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n},\quad\mathcal{V}=\bar{\mathbb{A}}(\bm{k}),

(D.18)

Condition (i) of Lemma
A.3
,
is verified by Proposition
D.2
,
and Condition (ii) is verified by Proposition
D.3
.
In particular,
in Condition (i) of Lemma
A.3
, note that

\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{d}(X_{n},Y^{\delta}_{n})\mathbbm{I}(X_{n}\in B\text{ or }Y^{\delta}_{n}\in B)>\Delta\big)\leq\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{d}(X_{n},Y^{\delta}_{n})>\Delta\big),

and the claim

\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{d}(X_{n},Y^{\delta}_{n})>\Delta\big)=\mathit{o}(\epsilon_{n})

as
n\to\infty
, under the choices in (
D.18
) with
\delta>0
small enough, is exactly the content of Proposition
D.2
.
As for Condition (ii), note that the claims are trivial when
B=\emptyset
.
In case that
B\neq\emptyset
, since
B
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}
, we pick
\Delta>0
small enough
B^{\Delta}
is still bounded away from
\mathbb{C}
.
By Urysohn’s Lemma, one can find some bounded and continuous
f:\mathbb{D}\to[0,1]
such that
\mathbbm{I}_{B}\leq f\leq\mathbbm{I}_{B^{\Delta}}
.
Then, by applying part (b) of Proposition
D.3
onto such
f
,
one can identify some
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(B,\Delta)>0
such that for all but countably many
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\in B,\ \bar{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}=(\bm{\mathcal{K}},0)\big)}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}\big[f(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n})\mathbbm{I}\{\bar{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}=(\bm{\mathcal{K}},0)\}\big]}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}=\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(f)\leq\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(B^{\Delta}),\qquad\forall\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k}).

By a similar argument using Urysohn’s Lemma, one can identify some bounded and continuous
f
with

\mathbbm{I}_{B_{\Delta}}\leq f\leq\mathbbm{I}_{B}
.
Using part (b) of Proposition
D.3
again (and by picking a smaller
\delta_{0}>0
if necessary), it follows for all but countably many
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
that

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\in B,\bar{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}=(\bm{\mathcal{K}},0)\big)}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}\geq\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(B_{\Delta}),\qquad\forall\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k}).

Likewise, by part (a) of of Proposition
D.3
,
for the continuous and bounded
f
with
\mathbbm{I}_{B}\leq f\leq\mathbbm{I}_{B^{\Delta}}
identified above,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\in B,\ \bar{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}\notin\bar{\mathbb{A}}(\bm{k})\big)}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}\big[f(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n})\mathbbm{I}\{\bar{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}\notin\bar{\mathbb{A}}(\bm{k})\}\big]}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}=0.

These calculations verify Condition (ii) of Lemma
A.3
under the choices in (
D.18
).
Using Lemma
A.3
, we conclude the proof of Theorem
3.2
.
∎

D.2
Proofs of Propositions
D.2
and
D.3

This subsection collects the proofs of Propositions
D.2
and
D.3
.
To this end, we prepare a few technical lemmas.
First, Lemma
D.4
establishes a concentration inequality for
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)
before the arrival of any large jump.

Lemma D.4
.

Let Assumption
5
hold.
Given any
\epsilon,\beta,T>0
, there exists
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\epsilon,\beta)
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{-\beta}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,T]:\ t<\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(1)}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}t\right\rVert>\epsilon\Bigg)=0\qquad\forall\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we work with the assumption that
\mu_{\bm{L}}=\bm{0}
and
T=1
.
Recall the Lévy–Itô decomposition stated in (
3.1
):

\displaystyle\bm{L}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\underbrace{\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{L}}^{1/2}\bm{B}(t)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\bm{L}_{1}(t)}+\underbrace{\int_{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert\leq 1}\bm{x}\big[\text{PRM}_{\nu}([0,t]\times d\bm{x})-t\nu(dx)\big]}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\bm{L}_{2}(t)}+\underbrace{\bm{c}_{\bm{L}}t+\int_{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>1}\bm{x}\text{PRM}_{\nu}([0,t]\times d\bm{x})}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\bm{L}_{3}(t)},

where
\nu
is the Lévy measure supported on
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}

satisfying
\int_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}}(\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert^{2}\wedge 1)\nu(d\bm{x})<\infty
.
By definitions in (
D.2
),
\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(1)
is the arrival time of the first discontinuity in
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)
with norm larger than
\delta
.
Therefore, after the
1/n
time-scaling,
n\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(1)
is the arrival time of the first discontinuity in
\bm{L}(t)
larger than
n\delta
.
Then, it suffices to show that (as
n\to\infty
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,n]}\left\lVert\bm{L}_{1}(t)\right\rVert>n\epsilon/3\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),

(D.19)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,n]}\left\lVert\bm{L}_{2}(t)\right\rVert>n\epsilon/3\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),

(D.20)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,n]:\ t<n\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(1)}\left\lVert\bm{L}_{3}(t)\right\rVert>n\epsilon/3\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough}.

(D.21)

Proof of Claim (
D.19
)
.
We use
\sigma_{i,j}^{1/2}
to denote the element in the
i^{\text{th}}
row and
j^{\text{th}}
column of the positive semi-definite matrix
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{L}}^{1/2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
,
and write
\bm{B}(t)=\big(B_{1}(t),\ldots,B_{d}(t)\big)^{\text{T}}
.
Let
C\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\max_{i,j\in[d]}|\sigma_{i,j}^{1/2}|
.
Suppose that

\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|B_{j}(t)|\leq\frac{n\epsilon}{3Cd^{2}}

holds for each
j\in[d]
.
Then, under the
L_{1}
norm,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,n]}\left\lVert\bm{L}_{1}(t)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{j\in[d]}|\sigma^{1/2}_{i,j}|\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|B_{j}(t)|\leq\sum_{j\in[d]}\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|B_{j}(t)|\cdot Cd\leq d\cdot\frac{n\epsilon}{3Cd^{2}}\cdot Cd\leq\epsilon/3.

Therefore, it suffices to show that

\mathbf{P}\big(\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|B(t)|>\frac{n\epsilon}{3Cd^{2}}\big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta})

holds for a standard Brownian motion
B(t)
in
\mathbb{R}^{1}
.
By Doob’s maximal inequality and the MGF of
B(t)
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|B(t)|>\frac{n\epsilon}{3Cd^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot\exp\bigg[-\frac{1}{2n}\cdot\bigg(\frac{n\epsilon}{3Cd^{2}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]=2\cdot\exp\bigg(-n\cdot\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{18C^{2}d^{4}}\bigg)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),

and conclude the proof of Claim (
D.19
).

Proof of Claim (
D.20
)
.
Note that each coordinate of
\bm{L}_{2}(t)=(L_{2,1}(t),\ldots,L_{2,d}(t))^{\top}
is a Lévy process with bounded jumps and also a martingale.
Therefore, each
L_{2,j}(t)
has finite moments of any order;
see Theorem 34 in Chapter I of
[
58
]
.
Meanwhile, by
Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequalities (see Theorem 48 in Chapter IV of
[
58
]
),
for each
p\in[1,\infty)
, there exists some constant
c_{p}\in(0,\infty)
(whose value does not vary with the law of
\bm{L}
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|L_{2,j}(t)|^{p}\bigg]\leq c_{p}\mathbf{E}\bigg[\Big([L_{2,j},L_{2,j}](n)\Big)^{p/2}\bigg],\qquad\forall j\in[d],\ n\geq 1,

where
V_{j}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}[L_{2,j},L_{2,j}](t)
is the quadratic variation process of
L_{2,j}(t)
.
Due to the independent and stationary increments of Lévy processes
L_{2,j}(t)
,
the quadratic variation
V_{j}(t)=[L_{2,j},L_{2,j}](t)
also has stationary and independent increments.
Then, by the Minkowski inequality,
for each
p\in[1,\infty)
we have

\displaystyle\Bigg(\mathbf{E}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|L_{2,j}(t)|^{p}\bigg]\Bigg)^{2/p}

\displaystyle\leq(c_{p})^{2/p}\cdot\Bigg(\mathbf{E}\bigg[\Big([L_{2,j},L_{2,j}](n)\Big)^{p/2}\bigg]\Bigg)^{2/p}

\displaystyle\leq(c_{p})^{2/p}\cdot n\cdot\Big(\underbrace{\mathbf{E}\big[\big(V_{j}(1)\big)^{p/2}\big]}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}v_{p,j}<\infty}\Big)^{2/p}.

As a result, for each
j\in[d]
,
p\in[1,\infty)
, and
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|L_{2,j}(t)|>\frac{n\epsilon}{3d}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{3d}{\epsilon}\bigg)^{p}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{E}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|L_{2,j}(t)|^{p}\big]}{n^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{3d}{\epsilon}\bigg)^{p}\cdot\frac{n^{p/2}\cdot c_{p}v_{p,j}}{n^{p}}=\bigg(\frac{3d}{\epsilon}\bigg)^{p}\cdot\frac{c_{p}v_{p,j}}{n^{p/2}}.

Picking
p>2\beta
,
we conclude the proof of Claim (
D.20
).

Proof of Claim (
D.21
)
.
Note that
\bm{L}_{3}(t)
is a compound Poisson process with drift, i.e.,

\bm{L}_{3}(t)=\bm{c}_{\bm{L}}t+\sum_{i=1}^{N(t)}\bm{Z}_{n},

where
N(t)
is a Poisson process with rate
\lambda=\nu\big(\{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>1\}\big)
,
and the

\bm{Z}_{n}
’s are i.i.d. copies under the law

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\bm{Z}\in\ \cdot\ )=\frac{\nu\big(\ \cdot\ \cap\{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>1\}\big)}{\nu\{\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>1\}}.

In particular, under our running assumption
\mu_{\bm{L}}=\bm{0}
,
by (
3.4
)
we have
\bm{c}_{\bm{L}}=-\lambda\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}
for the linear drift, and hence

\displaystyle\bm{L}_{3}(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{N(t)}\bm{Z}_{n}-t\cdot\lambda\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}.

Also, by Assumption
5
and the
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition in Definition
2.1
,

\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}\right\rVert>n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha(\{i^{*}\})}(n)
with
\alpha(\{i^{*}\})>1
,
where
i^{*}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\arg\min_{i\in[d]}\alpha(\{i\})
.
This confirms that
\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert<\infty
and
\left\lVert\bm{Z}\right\rVert
has regularly varying law with tail index larger than 1.

To proceed, let
j^{*}=j^{*}(n,\delta)
be the index of the first
\bm{Z}_{j}
with
\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{j}\right\rVert>n\delta
,
and
\tau^{*}=\tau^{*}(n,\delta)
be its arrival time in the compound Poisson process
\sum_{i=1}^{N(t)}\bm{Z}_{n}
.
Given
\hat{\epsilon}>0
, on the event

\big\{\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|N(t)-\lambda t|\leq n\hat{\epsilon}\big\},

observe that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,n]:\ t<n\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(1)}\left\lVert\bm{L}_{3}(t)\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,n]:\ t<\tau^{*}}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{N(t)}\bm{Z}_{n}-t\cdot\lambda\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,n]:\ t<\tau^{*}}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{N(t)}\bm{Z}_{n}-N(t)\cdot\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert+\sup_{t\in[0,n]:\ t<\tau^{*}}|N(t)-\lambda t|\cdot\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\leq(\lambda+\hat{\epsilon})\cdot n:\ j<j^{*}}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{j}\bm{Z}_{i}-i\cdot\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert+n\hat{\epsilon}\cdot\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\leq(\lambda+\hat{\epsilon})\cdot n}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{j}\bm{Z}_{i}\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert\leq n\delta\}-i\cdot\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert+n\hat{\epsilon}\cdot\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert.

In particular, by picking
\hat{\epsilon}>0
small enough, we get
\hat{\epsilon}\cdot\left\lVert\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert<\epsilon/6
.
Therefore, to prove Claim (
D.21
),
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,n]}|N(t)-\lambda t|>n\hat{\epsilon}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),

(D.22)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\max_{j\leq(\lambda+\hat{\epsilon})\cdot n}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{j}\bm{Z}_{i}\mathbbm{I}\{\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{i}\right\rVert\leq n\delta\}-i\cdot\mathbf{E}\bm{Z}\right\rVert>n\epsilon/6\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),\quad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough}.

(D.23)

However, the bound (
D.22
) follows from Doob’s maximal inequality and Cramer’s theorem,
and the bound (
D.23
)
follows from Lemma 3.1 in
[
72
]
(i.e., concentration inequalities for truncated regularly varying vectors).
This concludes the proof of Claim (
D.21
).
∎

The next result justifies the use of
\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}
in (
D.4
) as an approximator to
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}
.

Lemma D.5
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\epsilon>0
.
For any
n\geq 1
and
\delta>0
,
it holds on event

\displaystyle\big\{\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k)\leq 1<\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k+1)\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cap\Bigg(\bigcap_{m=1}^{k+1}\underbrace{\bigg\{\sup_{t\in[\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(m-1),1]:\ t<\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(m)}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)-\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}\Big(\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(m-1)\Big)\right\rVert\leq\frac{\epsilon}{k+1}\bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.23608pt\raisebox{4.3691pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.23608pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}A_{n}(m;\delta,k,\epsilon)}\Bigg)

that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\lVert\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)-\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)\right\rVert\leq\epsilon.

Proof.

For any
\xi,\tilde{\xi}\in\mathbb{D}
and any
0\leq u<v\leq 1
, observe the elementary bound

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,v]}\left\lVert\xi(t)-\tilde{\xi}(t)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,u]}\left\lVert\xi(t)-\tilde{\xi}(t)\right\rVert+\sup_{t\in[u,v)}\left\lVert\Big(\xi(t)-\xi(u)\Big)-\Big(\tilde{\xi}(t)-\tilde{\xi}(u)\Big)\right\rVert+\left\lVert\Delta\xi(v)-\Delta\tilde{\xi}(v)\right\rVert.

Furthermore, applying this bound inductively, it holds for any
0<t_{1}<t_{2}<\ldots<t_{k}\leq 1
that (under the convention that
t_{0}=0
and
t_{k+1}=1
)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\lVert\xi(t)-\tilde{\xi}(t)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m=1}^{k+1}\sup_{t\in[t_{m-1},t_{m})}\left\lVert\Big(\xi(t)-\xi(t_{m-1})\Big)-\Big(\tilde{\xi}(t)-\tilde{\xi}(t_{m-1})\Big)\right\rVert+\left\lVert\Delta\xi(t_{m})-\Delta\tilde{\xi}(t_{m})\right\rVert.

Now, on event

\big\{\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k)\leq 1<\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k+1)\big\}\cap\big(\bigcap_{m=1}^{k+1}A_{n}(m;\delta,k,\epsilon)\big),

we apply this bound with
\xi=\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}
,
\tilde{\xi}=\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}
, and

t_{m}=\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(m).

In particular, by (
D.4
),
we have

\sup_{t\in[t_{m-1},t_{m})}\left\lVert\tilde{\xi}(t)-\tilde{\xi}(t_{m-1})\right\rVert=0

and

\left\lVert\Delta\xi(t_{m})-\Delta\tilde{\xi}(t_{m})\right\rVert=0.

The claims then follow directly from the condition in the event
A_{n}(m;\delta,k,\epsilon)
.
∎

In Lemma
D.6
, we develop useful asymptotics for
{\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})}
and
{\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})}
.
Specifically, given
\delta>0
,
n\geq 1
,
we define the event

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}^{>\delta}_{n}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=0\big\}.

(D.24)

Furthermore, given

\mathcal{K}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}
, we define

\displaystyle E^{>\delta}_{n}(\mathcal{K})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=\mathcal{K}\big\}\cap\widetilde{E}^{>\delta}_{n}=\Bigg(\bigcap_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\underbrace{\Big\{K^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{j})=\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}\Big\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}E^{>\delta}_{n;\bm{j}}(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})}\Bigg)\cap\big\{\widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=0\big\}.

(D.25)

Given
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
and
c>0
,
let
\big(\bm{W}_{*}^{(c)}(k;\bm{j})\big)_{k\geq 1}

be sequences that are independent across
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
,
where each
\bm{W}_{*}^{(c)}(k;\bm{j})

is an i.i.d. copy of

\bm{W}_{*}^{(c)}(\bm{j})

with law

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{W}_{*}^{(c)}(\bm{j})\in\ \cdot\ \Big)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\Big(\ \cdot\ \cap\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>c}(\bm{j})\Big)\Big/\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\Big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>c}(\bm{j})\Big),

(D.26)

where the
\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}
’s are the limiting measures in the
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition of Assumption
5
,
and the
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>c}(\bm{j})
’s are defined in (
D.5
).
Besides, let the
U_{\bm{j},k}
’s be i.i.d. copies of Unif
(0,1)
,
and, for each
k\geq 1
and
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
,
let

U_{\bm{j},(1:k)}\leq U_{\bm{j},(2:k)}\leq\ldots\leq U_{\bm{j},(k:k)}

be the order statistics of
(U_{\bm{j},q})_{q\in[k]}
.

Lemma D.6
.

Let Assumptions
5
and
6
hold.
Let

\mathcal{K}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}
,
and let

\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{+}
satisfy (
3.13
) (i.e.,
\mathcal{K}
is an allocation of
\bm{k}
).
First,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\gamma}\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(\widetilde{E}^{>\delta}_{n}\big)^{c}\Big)=0,\qquad\forall\gamma>0,\ \delta>0.

(D.27)

Next,
for any
\delta>0

if Claim (
D.6
) holds, then

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(E^{>\delta}_{n}(\mathcal{K})\big)}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}=\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\frac{1}{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}!}\cdot\Big(\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)\Big)^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}},

(D.28)

where

\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{j}}(\cdot)

is defined in (
3.12
).
Furthermore,
if
\mathcal{K}\neq\bm{0}
, then

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}\Bigg(\Big(\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j}),\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})\Big)_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\Bigg|\ E^{>\delta}_{n}(\mathcal{K})\Bigg)\rightarrow\mathscr{L}\Bigg(\Big(U_{\bm{j},(k:\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})},\bm{W}^{(\delta)}_{*}(k;\bm{j})\Big)_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\Bigg)

(D.29)

as
n\to\infty

in terms of weak convergence.

Proof.

Recall the definition of
\nu_{n}(A)=\nu\{n\bm{x}:\ \bm{x}\in A\}
.
First, by the law of the (scaled) Lévy process
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)=\frac{1}{n}\bm{L}(nt)
in (
3.1
) and the definitions in (
D.12
)–(
D.13
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(\widetilde{E}^{>\delta}_{n}\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\text{Poisson}\Big(n\cdot\nu_{n}\big(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\setminus\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}([d],\delta)\big)\Big)\geq 1\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\text{Poisson}\Big(n\cdot\nu_{n}\big(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\setminus\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}([d],\delta)\big)\Big)\Bigg]=n\cdot\nu_{n}\bigg(\Big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}([d],\delta)\Big)^{c}\bigg)\ \text{by Markov's inequality}.

By the
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition in Assumption
5
(in particular, Claim (
2.6
) in Definition
2.1
),
we verify Claim (
D.27
).

Next,
by the independence when splitting the Poisson random measure
\text{PRM}_{\nu}
in (
3.1
),

\mathbf{P}\Big(E^{>\delta}_{n}(\mathcal{K})\Big)=\Bigg(\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathbf{P}\Big(E^{>\delta}_{n;\bm{j}}(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})\Big)\Bigg)\cdot\mathbf{P}\big(\widetilde{E}^{>\delta}_{n}\big).

On the one hand, Claim (
D.27
) implies that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(\widetilde{E}^{>\delta}_{n}\big)=1.

On the other hand, in light of property (
3.15
), to prove (
D.28
) it suffices to show that for any
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
and
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(E^{>\delta}_{n;\bm{j}}(k)\big)}{\big(n\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{k}}=\frac{1}{k!}\cdot\Big(\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)\Big)^{k}.

(D.30)

By the law of the (scaled) Lévy process
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)
in (
3.1
) and the definitions in (
D.8
)–(
D.9
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(E^{>\delta}_{n;\bm{j}}(k)\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\text{Poisson}\Big(n\cdot\underbrace{\nu_{n}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\theta_{n}(\bm{j},\delta)}\Big)=k\Bigg)=\exp\big(-n\theta_{n}(\bm{j},\delta)\big)\cdot\frac{\big(n\theta_{n}(\bm{j},\delta)\big)^{k}}{k!}.

(D.31)

By the
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition in Assumption
5
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\theta_{n}(\bm{j},\delta)}{\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)}=\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\Big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\Big);\qquad\text{see \eqref{cond, finite index, def: MRV} and our choice of $\delta$ in \eqref{proof: choice of c, bounded away set from cone i, Ld for Levy MRV}}.

This also implies
\theta_{n}(\bm{j},\delta)=\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)=\mathit{o}(n)
(due to
\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha(\bm{j})}(n)
with
\alpha(\bm{j})>1
),
and hence

\lim_{n\to\infty}\exp\big(-n\theta_{n}(\bm{j},\delta)\big)=1.

Plugging these limits back into (
D.31
), we conclude the proof of Claim (
D.30
).

Next, we prove the weak convergence stated in (
D.29
).
Again, by the independence of the Poisson splitting,
it suffices to show that for any
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
and
K\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}\bigg(\Big(\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j}),\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})\Big)_{k\in[K]}\bigg|\ E^{>\delta}_{n;\bm{j}}(K)\bigg)\rightarrow\mathscr{L}\bigg(\Big(U_{\bm{j},(k:K)},\bm{W}^{(\delta)}_{*}(k;\bm{j})\Big)_{k\in[K]}\bigg).

(D.32)

Next, by the law of a compound Poisson process,
the arrival times
\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})
are independent of the jump sizes
\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})
,
which implies that the law of the jump size sequence
\big(\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})\big)_{k\geq 1}
is independent of the event
E^{>\delta}_{n;\bm{j}}(K)
.
Furthermore,
the conditional law of
the
\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})
’s—the sequence of arrival times of jumps in a compound Poisson process conditioning on the number of jumps—admits the form

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}\bigg(\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(1;\bm{j}),\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(2;\bm{j}),\ldots,\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(K;\bm{j})\ \bigg|\ E^{>\delta}_{n;\bm{j}}(K)\bigg)=\mathscr{L}\bigg(U_{\bm{j},(1:K)},U_{\bm{j},(2:K)},\ldots,U_{\bm{j},(K:K)}\bigg).

Therefore, it only remains to study the marginal law for
\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(1;\bm{j})
(since all
\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})
’s admit the same law) and show that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(1;\bm{j})\in\ \cdot\ \Big)\rightarrow\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{W}^{(\delta)}_{*}(\bm{j})\in\ \cdot\ \Big)

in terms of weak convergence,
where the law of
\bm{W}^{(\delta)}_{*}(\bm{j})
is defined in (
D.26
).
To this end,
note that by the definitions in (
D.8
)–(
D.9
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(1;\bm{j})\in\ \cdot\ \Big)=\frac{\nu_{n}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\cap\ \cdot\ \big)}{\nu_{n}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)},\qquad\forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}.

(D.33)

Then, given a Borel set
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(1;\bm{j})\in A\Big)

\displaystyle=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\nu_{n}\Big(A\cap\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\Big)\Big/\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)}{\nu_{n}\Big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\Big)\Big/\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)}\qquad\text{by \eqref{proof: law of large jump in cone i, lemma: asymptotic law for large jumps, LD for Levy MRV}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\bigg(\Big(A\cap\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\Big)^{-}\bigg)\bigg/\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\bigg(\Big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\Big)^{\circ}\bigg)\qquad\text{by the $\mathcal{MHRV}$ condition and \eqref{cond, finite index, def: MRV}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\Big(A^{-}\cap\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\Big)\Big/\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\Big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\Big)\qquad\text{because of $(A\cap B)^{-}\subseteq A^{-}\cap B^{-}$ and \eqref{proof: choice of c, bounded away set from cone i, Ld for Levy MRV}
}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{W}^{(\delta)}_{*}(\bm{j})\in A^{-}\Big);\qquad\text{see \eqref{def: limiting law of large jumps, LD for levy, MRV}.}

Using the Portmanteau Theorem, we conclude the proof.
∎

Given
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}
and
\epsilon\geq 0
,
recall that any path
\xi\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}
admits the form

\displaystyle\xi(t)=\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},1]}(t),\qquad\forall t\in[0,1],

(D.34)

where
\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\in\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)
and
t_{\bm{j},k}\in(0,1]
for each
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
and
k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]
, and any two elements in the sequence
(t_{\bm{j},k})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}
will not coincide;
see (
3.7
) and (
3.8
).
Besides, recall that in Definition
3.1
,
we use
\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})
to denote the set of all allocations of
\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{+}
.
We prepare the next two lemmas to collect useful properties of sets
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\mathcal{K}}
and measures
\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathcal{K}}(\cdot)
(see (
3.10
)).

Lemma D.7
.

Let Assumption
5
hold.
Let
\bm{k}=(k_{j})_{j\in[d]}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{d}
with
\bm{k}\neq(0,\ldots,0)
,
\epsilon>0
,
and let
B
be a Borel set of
\mathbb{D}
equipped with Skorokhod
J_{1}
topology.
Suppose that
B
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}
under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}
for some
\epsilon>0
.
Then, there exist
\bar{\epsilon}>0
and
\bar{\delta}>0
such that the following claims hold:

1.

\bm{d}_{J_{1}}\big(B^{\bar{\epsilon}},\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}\big)>\bar{\epsilon}
;

2.

Given any
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})
and

\xi\in B^{\bar{\epsilon}}\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}
,
in the expression (
D.34
) for
\xi
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\right\rVert>\bar{\delta}\text{ and }\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\notin\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\bar{\delta}),\qquad\forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}],

(D.35)

where the set
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\epsilon)=\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\epsilon;\bar{\textbf{S}},\bm{\alpha})

is defined in (
2.4
).

Proof.

Part (a) follows directly from the fact that

B
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}
under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}
for some
\epsilon>0
, as well as the monotonicity of
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\subseteq\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}

for any
\epsilon\geq\epsilon^{\prime}\geq 0
and
\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}_{+}
.

Moving on to part (b),
since there are only finitely elements in
\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})
,
if suffices to fix some
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})

and prove the claim (
D.35
).
Specifically,
we take any
\bar{\delta}\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})

and proceed with a proof by contradiction.
Suppose that, for some
\xi\in B^{\bar{\epsilon}}\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}
,
there are
\bm{j}^{*}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
and
k^{*}\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}]
(which, of course, requires that
\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}\geq 1
)
such that

\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}\right\rVert\leq\bar{\delta}

in the expression for
\xi
in (
D.34
).
Then, with

\displaystyle\xi^{*}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}\setminus\{\bm{j}^{*}\}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},1]}(t)+\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}]:\ k\neq k^{*}}\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j}^{*},k},1]}(t),\qquad\forall t\in[0,1],

we construct a path
\xi^{*}
by removing the jump
\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}
from
\xi
.
By defining
\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\bm{j}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\begin{cases}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}&\text{ if }\bm{j}\neq\bm{j}^{*}\\
\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}-1&\text{ if }\bm{j}=\bm{j}^{*}\end{cases},

we have
\xi^{*}\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}}

and
\breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime})<\breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})
(see (
3.9
) and (
3.14
)),
thus implying

\xi^{*}\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}.

However, due to

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{J_{1}}(\xi,\xi^{*})\leq\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left\lVert\xi(t)-\xi^{*}(t)\right\rVert=\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}\right\rVert\leq\bar{\delta}<\bar{\epsilon}

and
\xi\in B^{\bar{\epsilon}}
,
we arrive at the contradiction that
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}(B^{\bar{\epsilon}},\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\leqslant\bm{k}})\leq\bar{\epsilon}
.
In summary, we have shown that

\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\right\rVert>\bar{\delta}

for any
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]
in the expression (
D.34
) for
\xi
.

Next,
suppose that, for some
\xi\in B^{\bar{\epsilon}}\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\mathcal{K}}
,
there exist
\bm{j}^{*}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
and
k^{*}\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}]
(which, again, requires that
\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}\geq 1
) such that
\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}\in\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j}^{*},\bar{\delta})
.
Then, by the definition of
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j}^{*},\bar{\delta})
in (
2.4
)
and our choice of
\bar{\delta}\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
,
there exists some
\bm{j}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
with

\bm{j}^{\prime}\neq\bm{j}^{*}
,
\alpha(\bm{j}^{\prime})\leq\alpha(\bm{j}^{*})
such that
\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}\in\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j}^{\prime},\bar{\delta})\subseteq\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j}^{\prime},\bar{\epsilon})
.
Now, define

\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\bm{j}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\begin{cases}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}-1&\text{ if }\bm{j}=\bm{j}^{*}\\
\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{\prime}}+1&\text{ if }\bm{j}=\bm{j}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases},

and note that
\xi\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}}
due to

\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}\in\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j}^{\prime},\bar{\epsilon})
.
Due to
\alpha(\bm{j}^{\prime})\leq\alpha(\bm{j}^{*})
,
we have

\breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime})\leq\breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})=c(\bm{k})
(see (
3.9
)),
and hence
\xi\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}
(see (
3.17
)).
In light of the running assumption
\xi\in B^{\bar{\epsilon}}
,
we arrive at the contradiction that

B^{\bar{\epsilon}}\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}\neq\emptyset
.
This concludes the proof of part (b).
∎

Lemma D.8
.

Let Assumption
5
hold.
Let
\bm{k}=(k_{j})_{j\in[d]}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{d}
such that
\bm{k}\neq\bm{0}
.
Let
T,\epsilon>0
and
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
For any Borel set
B
of
(\mathbb{D}[0,T],\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,T]}}})

that is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{x}}[0,T]
under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,T]}}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{x}}(B)<\infty.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we impose Assumption
6
(i.e., we focus on the case of
T=1
and
\bm{x}=\bm{0}
), and adopt the notations in (
D.1
).
Also,
since there are only finitely many elements in
\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})
,
it suffices to fix some
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})
and
some Borel set
B
of
(\mathbb{D},\bm{d}_{J_{1}})
that is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}
, and show that

\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(B)<\infty.

Since the measure
\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(\cdot)
is supported on

\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{0}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}
(see (
3.10
) and the remarks right after),
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\big(B\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{0}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\big)<\infty.

Since
B
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}
,
Lemma
D.7
allows us to fix some
\bar{\delta}>0
such that the following claim holds:
given any
\xi\in B\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{0}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}
,
in the expression (
D.34
) for
\xi
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\right\rVert>\bar{\delta}\text{ and }\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\notin\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\bar{\delta}),\qquad\forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}].

Then, by the definition of
\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}
in (
3.10
),

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\big(B\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{0}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}!}\cdot\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigg(\underbrace{\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\Big(\big\{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{w}\right\rVert>\bar{\delta},\ \bm{w}\notin\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\bar{\delta})\big\}\Big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}c_{\bm{j}}(\bar{\delta})}\bigg)^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}.

(D.36)

In particular, for any
\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})
, the set

\big\{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{w}\right\rVert>\bar{\delta},\ \bm{w}\notin\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\bar{\delta})\big\}

is bounded away from
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\delta)
.
Then, by the
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition in Assumption
5

(in particular, Claim (
2.5
) in Definition
2.1
)
we get

c_{\bm{j}}(\bar{\delta})<\infty
for each
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
.
Plugging these bounds back into (
D.36
), we conclude the proof.
∎

Now, we are ready to state the proof of Propositions
D.2
and
D.3
.

Proof of Proposition
D.2
.

Recall the definition of

c:\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{d}\to[0,\infty)
in (
3.11
) and

\breve{c}:\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\to[0,\infty)
in (
3.9
).
Define events

\displaystyle B_{0}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big\{\bm{d}_{J_{1}}\big(\hat{\bm{L}}_{n}^{>\delta},\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}\big)>\Delta\Big\},\quad B_{1}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big\{\widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=0\Big\},\quad B_{2}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big\{\breve{c}(\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n})\leq c({\bm{k}})\Big\}.

(D.37)

To establish Claim (
D.17
), it suffices to show that for any
\delta>0
small enough,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(B_{0}\setminus B_{1}\big)\big/\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)

\displaystyle=0,

(D.38)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\big((B_{0}\cap B_{1})\setminus B_{2}\big)\big/\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)

\displaystyle=0,

(D.39)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(B_{0}\cap B_{1}\cap B_{2}\big)\big/\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)

\displaystyle=0.

(D.40)

Proof of Claim (
D.38
)
.
By Claim (
D.27
) of Lemma
D.6
,
we get

\mathbf{P}\big((B_{1})^{c}\big)=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)\big)

as
n\to\infty
under any
\delta>0
.

Proof of Claim (
D.39
)
.
We prove this claim for any
\delta>0
.
Let

\mathbb{K}_{>}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big\{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}:\ \breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})>c({\bm{k}})\Big\}.

Note that

(B_{0}\cap B_{1})\setminus B_{2}\subseteq(B_{2})^{c}=\{\breve{c}(\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n})>c({\bm{k}})\}=\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}\in\mathbb{K}_{>}\}.

Meanwhile, by the definition of the linear function
\breve{c}(\cdot)
in (
3.9
)
with coefficients
\alpha(\bm{j})-1>0
for any
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
(see Assumption
5
),
there exists a subset
\mathbb{K}^{*}_{>}\subseteq\mathbb{K}_{>}

such that
|\mathbb{K}^{*}_{>}|<\infty
(i.e., containing only finitely many elements) and

\displaystyle\breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime})>c({\bm{k}})\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}\geq\bm{\mathcal{K}}\text{ for some }\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{K}^{*}_{>}.

Here,
the ordering
\bm{x}\geq\bm{y}
between two real vectors is equivalent to
x_{i}\geq y_{i}
for all
i
.
Then,

\big\{\breve{c}(\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n})>c({\bm{k}})\big\}\subseteq\bigcup_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{K}^{*}_{>}}\big\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}\geq\bm{\mathcal{K}}\big\}.

Therefore, to prove Claim (
D.39
),
it suffices to fix some
\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{K}^{*}_{>}

(or, more generally, some

\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}
such that
\breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})>c({\bm{k}})
)
and show that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}\geq\bm{\mathcal{K}}\big)\big/\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)

\displaystyle=0.

By the definition of
\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}
in (
D.11
) and the law of
\bm{L}(t)
in (
3.1
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}\geq\bm{\mathcal{K}}\big)

(D.41)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathbf{P}\bigg(K^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{j})\geq\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}\bigg)\quad\text{due to the independence of Poisson splitting}

\displaystyle=\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\text{Poisson}\Big(n\underbrace{\nu_{n}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\theta_{n}(\bm{j},\delta)}\Big)\geq\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}\bigg)\qquad\text{by \eqref{def: large jump in cone i, tau > delta n k i, LD for Levy MRV}--\eqref{def: K delta n type j, count scalar, LD for Levy}, where }\nu_{n}(A)=\nu\{n\bm{x}:\ \bm{x}\in A\}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\Big(n\theta_{n}(\bm{j},\delta)\Big)^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\qquad\text{due to }\mathbf{P}\big(\text{Poisson}(\lambda)\geq k\big)\leq\lambda^{k}\ \forall\lambda\geq 0.

Under the
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition in Assumption
5

(in particular, by Claim (
2.5
) in Definition
2.1
),
we have

\theta_{n}(\bm{j},\delta)=\mathcal{O}\big(\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)

for each
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
.
Plugging these bounds into display (
D.41
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}\geq\bm{\mathcal{K}}\big)=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\big(n\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\Bigg),\qquad\text{as }n\to\infty.

By the definition of
\breve{c}(\cdot)
in (
3.9
)
and
\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha(\bm{j})}(n)
,
we have

\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\big(n\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\big)^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})}(n).

Lastly, due to
\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-c(\bm{k})}(n)
(see (
3.12
)) and
\breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})>c(\bm{k})
,
we get

\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}\geq\bm{\mathcal{K}}\big)=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)\big).

This concludes the proof of Claim (
D.39
).

Proof of Claim (
D.40
)
.
We first note that the set

\displaystyle\mathbb{K}_{\leqslant}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}:\ \bm{\mathcal{K}}\notin\mathbb{A}(\bm{k}),\ \breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})\leq c(\bm{k})\big\}

contains only finitely many elements.
Therefore,
it suffices to fix some
\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{K}_{\leqslant}
and show that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{\bm{d}_{J_{1}}\big(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n},\ \hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\big)>\Delta\big\}\cap\big\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=\bm{\mathcal{K}},\ \widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=0\big\}\Big)\Big/\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)=0,\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough.}

Let events
A_{n}(m;\delta,k,\Delta)
be defined as in Lemma
D.5
,
and let
|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|=\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}
.
By Lemma
D.5
,

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{J_{1}}\big(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n},\ \hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\big)\leq\Delta\text{ holds on the event }\big\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=\bm{\mathcal{K}},\ \widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=0\big\}\cap\big(\cap_{m=1}^{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|+1}A_{n}(m;\delta,|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|,\Delta)\big).

(D.42)

Then, it only remains to show that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(\bigcap_{m=1}^{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|+1}A_{n}(m;\delta,|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|,\Delta)\big)^{c}\Big)\Big/\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)=0.

By the strong Markov property of the Lévy process
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}
at stopping times
\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(m)

defined in (
D.2
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\Big(\bigcap_{m=1}^{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|+1}A_{n}(m;\delta,|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|,\Delta)\Big)^{c}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq(|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|+1)\cdot\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,1]:\ t<\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(1)}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)\right\rVert>\frac{\Delta}{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|+1}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathit{o}\Big(\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)\Big)\qquad\text{ as $n\to\infty$ for any $\delta>0$ small enough.}

The last line follows from Lemma
D.4
.
This concludes the proof of
Claim (
D.40
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
D.3
.

(a) Note that

\displaystyle f(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n})\mathbbm{I}\big\{\bar{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}\notin\bar{\mathbb{A}}(\bm{k})\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert\cdot\mathbbm{I}\big\{\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\in B\}\mathbbm{I}\big\{\bar{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}\notin\bar{\mathbb{A}}(\bm{k})\big\}\qquad\text{due to $B=\text{supp}(f)$}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert\cdot\underbrace{\mathbbm{I}\big\{\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\in B\}\mathbbm{I}\big\{\widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}\geq 1\big\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}I_{1}(n,\delta)}+\left\lVert f\right\rVert\cdot\underbrace{\mathbbm{I}\big\{\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\in B\}\mathbbm{I}\big\{\breve{c}(\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n})>c(\bm{k})\big\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}I_{2}(n,\delta)}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\lVert f\right\rVert\cdot\underbrace{\mathbbm{I}\big\{\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\in B\}\mathbbm{I}\big\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}\notin\mathbb{A}(\bm{k}),\ \breve{c}(\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n})\leq c(\bm{k}),\ \widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=0\big\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}I_{3}(n,\delta)}.

Repeating the proof of Claims (
D.38
) and (
D.39
)
for Proposition
D.2
,
we get

\mathbf{E}\big[I_{1}(n,\delta)\big]=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)\big)

and

\mathbf{E}\big[I_{2}(n,\delta)\big]=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)\big)

(as
n\to\infty
) for any
\delta>0
.
Next, let

\mathbb{K}_{<}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}:\ \bm{\mathcal{K}}\notin\mathbb{A}(\bm{k}),\ \breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})\leq c(\bm{k})\big\}.

By the definition of

\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}=\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{0}}[0,1]
in (
3.19
),
for any
\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{K}_{<}
we have
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\delta}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}\subset\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\delta}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}
.
Therefore,
given
\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{K}_{<}
,
it holds on the event
\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=\bm{\mathcal{K}},\ \widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=0\}
that

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{L}}_{n}^{>\delta}\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{K}}\subseteq\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}},\qquad\text{due to property~\eqref{property: approximation hat L n when type = j, LD for Levy MRV} and our choice of $\delta\in(0,\epsilon)$.}

Since
B
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k}}
under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}

we must have
\big\{\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\in B\}\cap\big\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}\notin\mathbb{A}(\bm{k}),\ \breve{c}(\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n})\leq c(\bm{k})\big\}=\emptyset
.
In summary, we have shown that
I_{3}(n,\delta)=0
for each
\delta\in(0,\epsilon)
and
n\geq 1
.
This concludes the proof of part (a).

(b)
Recall that there are only finitely many assignments for
\bm{k}
(i.e.,
|\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})|<\infty
).
Therefore, it suffices to prove part (b) for some fixed
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})
.
By the definition of
B=\text{supp}(f)
, we have

\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(f)\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert\cdot\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(B).

Using Lemma
D.8
, we confirm that

\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(f)<\infty.

Next,
applying Lemma
D.7
onto
B=\text{supp}(f)
, one can fix some
\bar{\epsilon},\ \bar{\delta}>0
such that

\bm{d}_{J_{1}}(B^{\bar{\epsilon}},\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\leqslant\bm{k}})>\bar{\epsilon}

and, given
\xi\in B^{\bar{\epsilon}}\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\bar{\epsilon}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}
,
in the expression (
D.34
) for
\xi
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\right\rVert>\bar{\delta}\text{ and }\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\notin\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\bar{\delta}),\qquad\forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}].

(D.43)

To proceed, let

\displaystyle I_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(n,\delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}f(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n})\mathbbm{I}\big\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=\bm{\mathcal{K}},\ \widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=0\big\}.

Note that

\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=\bm{\mathcal{K}},\ \widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=0\big\}=E^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})
;
see (
D.25
).
Also, note that Claim (
D.6
) holds for all but countably many
\delta>0
, which is verified by Lemma
D.1
.
Henceforth in this proof, we only consider
\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})
such that Claim (
D.6
) holds.
Next,
let
|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}
,
and define the mapping
h(\cdot)
as follows:
given

\textbf{W}=(\bm{w}_{1},\ldots,\bm{w}_{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+})^{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|}

and

\bm{t}=(t_{1},\ldots,t_{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|})\in(0,1]^{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|},

let

\displaystyle h(\textbf{W},\bm{t})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{k=1,2,\ldots,|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|}\bm{w}_{k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{k},1]}.

(D.44)

One can see that
h:(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+})^{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|}\times(0,1]^{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|}\to\mathbb{D}
is continuous (w.r.t. the
J_{1}
topology of
\mathbb{D}
) when restricted on the domain

(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+})^{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|}\times(0,1)^{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|,*},

with

A^{k,*}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k})\in A^{k}:\ t_{i}\neq t_{j}\ \forall i,j\in[d]\text{ with }i\neq j\big\}.

This allows us to apply the continuous mapping theorem and study the asymptotic law of
f(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n})
conditioned on the event

E^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{\mathcal{K}}).

Specifically,
on
\{\bm{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=\bm{\mathcal{K}},\ \widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}=0\}
, we write

\displaystyle\textbf{W}^{>\delta}_{n}

\displaystyle=\Big(\bm{W}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})\Big)_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]},\qquad\textbf{T}^{>\delta}_{n}=\Big(\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})\Big)_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}.

By (
D.14
), on the event

E^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})

we have

\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}=h\big(\textbf{W}^{>\delta}_{n},\textbf{T}^{>\delta}_{n}\big).

As a result,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{E}\big[I_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(n,\delta)\big]}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}=\mathbf{E}\Big[f\Big(h\big(\textbf{W}^{>\delta}_{n},\textbf{T}^{>\delta}_{n}\big)\Big)\ \Big|\ E^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})\Big]\cdot\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(E^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})\big)}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}.

Then, by Lemma
D.6
and the continuity of
f\big(h(\cdot)\big)
on
(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+})^{|\mathcal{K}|}\times(0,1)^{|\mathcal{K}|,*}
, we obtain the following results
(note that
f(\xi)=0\ \forall\xi\notin B
), where we use the
U_{\bm{j},k}
’s to denote i.i.d. copies of Unif
(0,1)
,

U_{\bm{j},(1:k)}\leq U_{\bm{j},(2:k)}\leq\ldots\leq U_{\bm{j},(k:k)}

for the order statistics of
(U_{\bm{j},q})_{q\in[k]}
,

\mathcal{L}_{I}
for the Lebesgue measure restricted on interval
I
,
and
\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}(\cdot)
for the location measure in the
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition of Assumption
5
supported on
\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j})
(see Definition
2.1
):

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{E}\bigg[f\Big(h\big(\textbf{W}^{>\delta}_{n},\textbf{T}^{>\delta}_{n}\big)\Big)\ \bigg|\ E^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})\bigg]

\displaystyle=\int f\Bigg(\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},1]}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbbm{I}\Bigg\{\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},1]}\in B\Bigg\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\in\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\ \forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Bigg(\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\frac{\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}(d\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k})}{\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)}\Bigg)\Bigg(\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Big(U_{\bm{j},(1:\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})},\ldots,U_{\bm{j},(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}:\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})}\Big)\in d(t_{\bm{j},1},\ldots,t_{\bm{j},\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}})\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{=}}\int f\Bigg(\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},1]}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbbm{I}\Bigg\{\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},1]}\in B\Bigg\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}\Big\{\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\notin\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\delta)\ \forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Bigg(\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\frac{\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}(d\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k})}{\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)}\Bigg)\Bigg(\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Big(U_{\bm{j},(1:\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})},\ldots,U_{\bm{j},(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}:\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})}\Big)\in d(t_{\bm{j},1},\ldots,t_{\bm{j},\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}})\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\triangle)}}{{=}}\int f\Bigg(\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},1]}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Bigg(\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\frac{\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}(d\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k})}{\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)}\Bigg)\Bigg(\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Big(U_{\bm{j},(1:\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})},\ldots,U_{\bm{j},(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}:\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})}\Big)\in d(t_{\bm{j},1},\ldots,t_{\bm{j},\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}})\bigg)\Bigg).

In the display above, the step
(*)
holds since
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})=\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\delta)\setminus\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\delta)
, and
\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}(\cdot)
is supported on
\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j})\subseteq\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\delta)
;
the step
(\triangle)
follows from the definition of
B=\text{supp}(f)
,
our choice of
\bar{\delta}
in (
D.43
) and
\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})
.
To proceed, we make a few observations.
First, using
U_{(1:k)}<U_{(2:k)}<\ldots<U_{(k:k)}
to denote the order statistics of
k
copies of Unif
(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big((U_{(1:k)},U_{(2:k)},\ldots,U_{(k:k)})\in d(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k})\Big)=k!\mathbbm{I}\{0<t_{1}<t_{2}<\ldots<t_{k}<1\}dt_{1}dt_{2}\ldots dt_{k}.

Second, the mapping
h
defined in (
D.44
) is invariant under permutation of its arguments.
That is, given
\textbf{W}=(\bm{w}_{1},\ldots,\bm{w}_{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|})
and
\bm{t}=(t_{1},\ldots,t_{|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|})
,
we have

h(\textbf{W},\bm{t})=h\Big((\bm{w}_{\sigma(i)})_{i=1,\ldots,|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|},\ (t_{\sigma(i)})_{i=1,\ldots,|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|}\Big)

for any permutation
(\sigma(i))_{i=1,\ldots,|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|}
of
\{1,2,\ldots,|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|\}
.
These two properties allow us to re-evaluate step
(\triangle)
as an integral over the domain

\{(t_{\bm{j},k})_{\bm{j},k}:\ t_{\bm{j},k}\in(0,1)\forall\bm{j},k\}

instead of imposing the ordering constraint

t_{\bm{j},1}<t_{\bm{j},2}<\ldots<t_{\bm{j},\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}

for each
\bm{j}
.
Specifically,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{E}\bigg[f\Big(h\big(\textbf{W}^{>\delta}_{n},\textbf{T}^{>\delta}_{n}\big)\Big)\ \bigg|\ E^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})\bigg]

(D.45)

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigg(\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)\bigg)^{-\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\int f\Bigg(\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},1]}\Bigg)\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bigg(\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\times\mathcal{L}_{(0,1)}\Big(d(\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k},t_{\bm{j},k})\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigg(\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)\bigg)^{-\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\Bigg]\cdot\Bigg({\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}!}\Bigg)\cdot\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\mathcal{K}}(f)\qquad\text{ by definitions in \eqref{def: measure C type j L, LD for Levy, MRV} and \eqref{proof, def: shorthand, LD for Levy}}.

On the other hand, applying the claim (
D.28
) in Lemma
D.6
,
we get

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(E^{>\delta}_{n}(\mathcal{K})\big)}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}=\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\frac{1}{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}!}\cdot\Big(\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\big(\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\big)\Big)^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}.

(D.46)

Combining (
D.45
) and (
D.46
), we conclude the proof.
∎

Appendix E
Proof for Large Deviations of Multivariate Heavy-Tailed Hawkes Processes

E.1
Adapting Theorem
3.2
to
\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)

We first establish sample path large deviations for Lévy processes with
\mathcal{MHRV}
increments (i.e., a suitable modification of Theorem
3.2
) w.r.t. the
J_{1}
topology on
\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)
.
Specifically,
recall that the projection mapping

{\phi_{t}}:\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{D}[0,t]
is defined by
\phi_{t}(\xi)(s)=\xi(s)
for any
s\in[0,t]
,
and
let

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}(\xi^{(1)},\xi^{(2)})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\cdot\Big[\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}\Big(\phi_{t}\big(\xi^{(1)}),\ \phi_{t}\big(\xi^{(2)}\big)\Big)\wedge 1\Big]dt,\qquad\forall\xi^{(1)},\xi^{(2)}\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty).

(E.1)

We need the following two lemmas.
First, Lemma
E.1
adapts Lemmas
D.7
and
D.8
to
\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)
.

Lemma E.1
.

Let Assumption
5
hold.
Let

\bm{k}=(k_{j})_{j\in[d]}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{d}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
,
and
\epsilon>0
.
Given any Borel set
B
of
(\mathbb{D}[0,\infty),\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}})
that is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)
under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}
,

1.

there exist
T\in(0,\infty)
and
\bar{\delta}\in(0,\infty)
such that the following claim holds:
for any

\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})

and

\xi\in B\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)
, in the expression (
3.8
) for
\xi
(i.e., under
I=[0,\infty)
and
\bm{x}=\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}
) , we have

\displaystyle t_{\bm{j},k}\leq T,\quad\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\right\rVert>\bar{\delta},\text{ and }\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\notin\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\bar{\delta}),\qquad\qquad\forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}];

(E.2)

2.

\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}(B)<\infty.

Proof.

Fix some
\Delta>0
such that

\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(B,\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)\big)>\Delta.

(i)

Since
|\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})|<\infty
,
it suffices to prove part
(i)
for some fixed
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})
.
We first show that Claim (
E.2
) holds for any
T>0
large enough such that

\displaystyle e^{-T}<\Delta.

(E.3)

We consider a proof by contradiction.
Suppose that for some
\xi\in B\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)
, in expression (
3.8
)
there exist some
\bm{j}^{*}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
and
k^{*}\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}]
such that
t_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}>T
.
By defining

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}t+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}\{t_{\bm{j},k}\leq T\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},\infty)}(t),\qquad\forall t\geq 0,

we construct a path
\xi^{\prime}
by removing any jump in
\xi
that arrives after time
T
.
On the one hand, the existence of
t_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}>T
implies that we are removing at least one jump when defining
\xi^{\prime}
,
and hence

\xi^{\prime}\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)

by definitions in (
3.17
).
On the other hand, since
\xi(t)=\xi^{\prime}(t)
for each
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}(\xi,\xi^{\prime})\leq\int_{t>T}e^{-t}dt=e^{-T}<\Delta.

Due to
\xi\in B
, we arrive at the contradiction that
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(B,\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)\big)<\Delta,

which verifies claim (
E.2
) under our choice of
T
in (
E.3
).

Next,
through a proof by contradiction,
we show that Claim (
E.2
) holds under

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}\in(0,\epsilon\wedge\Delta).

(E.4)

First, suppose that for some
\xi\in B\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)
,
in expression (
3.8
)
there exist some
\bm{j}^{*}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
and
k^{*}\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}]
such that
\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}\right\rVert\leq\bar{\delta}
.
By defining

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}t+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}\Big\{(\bm{j},k)\neq(\bm{j}^{*},k^{*})\Big\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},\infty)}(t),\qquad\forall t\geq 0,

we construct a path
\xi^{\prime}
by removing the jump
\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}
—arriving at time
t_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}
—from
\xi
.
Again, we have

\xi^{\prime}\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty).

On the other hand, due to
\left\lVert\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}\right\rVert\leq\bar{\delta}
,
we have
\sup_{t\geq 0}\left\lVert\xi(t)-\xi^{\prime}(t)\right\rVert\leq\bar{\delta}
, and hence

\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}(\xi,\xi^{\prime})\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\cdot\bar{\delta}dt=\bar{\delta}<\Delta;

see (
E.4
).
We then arrive at the contradiction that

\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(B,\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)\big)<\Delta.

Second,
suppose that for some
\xi\in B\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)
, in expression (
3.8
)
there exist some
\bm{j}^{*}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
and
k^{*}\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}]
such that
{\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}}\in\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j}^{*},\bar{\delta})\subseteq\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j}^{*},\epsilon)

(due to our choice of
\bar{\delta}<\epsilon
in (
E.4
)).
Then, by definitions in (
2.4
),
there exists
some
\bm{j}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
with

\bm{j}^{\prime}\neq\bm{j}^{*}
,
\alpha(\bm{j}^{\prime})\leq\alpha(\bm{j}^{*})
such that
\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}\in\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j}^{\prime},\epsilon)
.
Now, define

\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}=(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\prime}_{\bm{j}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\begin{cases}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{*}}-1&\text{ if }\bm{j}=\bm{j}^{*}\\
\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}^{\prime}}+1&\text{ if }\bm{j}=\bm{j}^{\prime}\\
\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}.

First,
\bm{w}_{\bm{j}^{*},k^{*}}\in\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j}^{\prime},\epsilon)
implies that

\xi\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty).

Moreover, due to
\alpha(\bm{j}^{\prime})\leq\alpha(\bm{j}^{*})
,
we have
\breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime})\leq\breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})=c(\bm{k})
,
and hence

\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)\subseteq\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty).

By
\xi\in B
,
we now arrive at

B\cap\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)\neq\emptyset,

which clearly contradicts
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(B,\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)\big)>\Delta.

This concludes the proof of claim (
E.2
) under our choice of
\bar{\delta}
in (
E.4
).

(ii)

Again, it suffices to fix some
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})

and show that

\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}(B)<\infty.

Let
T
and
\bar{\delta}
be characterized as in part
(i)
.
By the definition of
\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}
in (
3.10
),

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{C}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}(B)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}!}\cdot\int\mathbbm{I}\Bigg\{t_{\bm{j},k}\leq T,\ \left\lVert\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\right\rVert>\bar{\delta},\text{ and }\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\notin\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\bar{\delta}),\quad\forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bigg((\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\times\mathcal{L}_{(0,\infty)})\Big(d(\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k},t_{\bm{j},k})\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}!}\cdot\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigg(T\cdot\underbrace{\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\Big(\big\{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{w}\right\rVert>\bar{\delta},\ \bm{w}\notin\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\bar{\delta})\big\}\Big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}c_{\bm{j}}(\bar{\delta})}\bigg)^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}.

Furthermore, it has been shown in the proof of Lemma
D.8
that
c_{\bm{j}}(\bar{\delta})<\infty
.
This concludes the proof of part
(ii)
.
∎

Recall the definition of the projection mapping

{\phi_{t}}:\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{D}[0,t]
in (
3.23
),
i.e.,
\phi_{t}(\xi)(s)=\xi(s)
for any
s\in[0,t]
.
Lemma
E.2
connects the bounded-away-from-
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{x}}[0,\infty)
condition (under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}
) to that of

\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{x}}[0,T]
and
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,T]}}}
.

Lemma E.2
.

Let

\bm{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{d}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
,

\epsilon>0
, and
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Given a Borel set
B
of
(\mathbb{D}[0,\infty),\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}})
that is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{x}}[0,\infty)
under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}
,
it holds for any
T>0
large enough that

\phi_{T}(B)

is bounded away from

\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{x}}[0,T]
under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}
.

Proof.

Fix some
\Delta>0
such that

\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(B,\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{x}}[0,\infty)\big)>\Delta.

Using a proof by contradiction,
we show that the claim holds for any
T>0
large enough such that

\displaystyle e^{-T}<\Delta/2.

(E.5)

Specifically,
suppose there are sequences
\xi_{n}\in\phi_{T}(B)
,
\xi^{\prime}_{n}\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{x}}[0,T]

such that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}(\xi_{n},\xi^{\prime}_{n})=0.

(E.6)

Then,
considering the definition of
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{x}}[0,T]
in (
3.17
)
and
by picking a sub-sequence if needed,
we can w.l.o.g. assume the existence of
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}
such that

\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k}),\ \breve{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})\leq c(\bm{k})
,
and

\xi^{\prime}_{n}\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{x}}[0,T]

for each
n\geq 1
.
In particular, due to
\bm{k}\neq\bm{0}
, we must have
\bm{\mathcal{K}}\neq\bm{0}
.
Note that

|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}

is the number of jumps for any path in
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{x}}[0,T]
.
Since
\xi^{\prime}_{n}\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{x}}[0,T]
for each
n
,
by (
3.8
) we know that
\xi^{\prime}_{n}
admits the form

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime}_{n}(t)=t\bm{x}+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\bm{w}^{(n)}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t^{(n)}_{\bm{j},k},T]}(t),\qquad\forall t\in[0,T],

where
each
\bm{w}^{(n)}_{\bm{j},k}
belongs to the cone
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)
.
Next, we fix some

\displaystyle\delta\in\bigg(0,\frac{\Delta}{2+4\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert+4|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|}\bigg).

(E.7)

By (
E.6
),
it holds for any
n
large enough that we have

\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}(\xi_{n},\xi^{\prime}_{n})<\delta.

In other words, for each
n
large enough there exists
\lambda_{n}
—a homeomorphism on
[0,T]
—such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\lambda_{n}(t)-t|\vee\left\lVert\xi^{\prime}_{n}\big(\lambda_{n}(t)\big)-\xi_{n}(t)\right\rVert<\delta.

(E.8)

Under such large
n
,
we consider
t\in(0,T)
such that

\displaystyle t\notin[t^{(n)}_{\bm{j},k}-\delta,t^{(n)}_{\bm{j},k}+\delta],\qquad\forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}].

(E.9)

By (
E.9
),
\xi^{\prime}_{n}(\cdot)
does note make any jump over the interval
[t-\delta,t+\delta]\cap[0,T]
,
meaning that, over this interval, the path
\xi^{\prime}_{n}(\cdot)
is a linear function with slope
\bm{x}
.
Then, by (
E.8
), we get

\displaystyle\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in[t-\delta,t+\delta]\cap[0,T]}\left\lVert\xi_{n}(t_{1})-\xi^{\prime}_{n}(t_{2})\right\rVert\leq\delta+\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert\cdot 2\delta.

Therefore, by modifying
\lambda_{n}(\cdot)
only over
[t-\delta,t]
,
one can obtain
\hat{\lambda}^{(t)}_{n}
—a homeomorphism over
[0,t]
—such that

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in[0,t]}|\hat{\lambda}^{(t)}_{n}(u)-u|\vee\left\lVert\xi^{\prime}_{n}\big(\hat{\lambda}^{(t)}_{n}(u)\big)-\xi_{n}(u)\right\rVert<\delta\cdot(1+2\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert).

Applying this bound on
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}(\phi_{t}(\xi_{n}),\phi_{t}(\xi^{\prime}_{n}))\wedge 1
for any
t\in(0,T)
satisfying (
E.9
),
and applying the trivial upper bound
1
for any
t
where (
E.9
) doest not hold,
we get (for all
n
large enough)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}e^{-t}\cdot\Big[\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}\Big(\phi_{t}\big(\xi_{n}),\ \phi_{t}\big(\xi^{\prime}_{n}\big)\Big)\wedge 1\Big]dt

(E.10)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t\in[0,T]:\ t\notin[t^{(n)}_{\bm{j},k}-\delta,t^{(n)}_{\bm{j},k}+\delta]\ \forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}e^{-t}\cdot\delta\cdot(1+2\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert)dt+|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|\cdot 2\delta

\displaystyle\leq\delta\cdot\big(1+2\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert+2|\bm{\mathcal{K}}|\big)<\Delta/2\qquad\text{by \eqref{proof, choice of delta, lemma: bounded away condition under J1, from infty to T}}.

Furthermore, since
\xi_{n}\in\phi_{t}(B)
, for each
n\geq 1
there is
\tilde{\xi}_{n}\in B
such that
\tilde{\xi}_{n}(t)=\xi_{n}(t)\ \forall t\in[0,T]
.
Also, by extending the path
\xi^{\prime}_{n}
linearly with slope
\bm{x}
over
(T,\infty)
,
we obtain
\tilde{\xi}^{\prime}_{n}\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{x}}[0,\infty)

such that
\tilde{\xi}^{\prime}_{n}(t)=\xi^{\prime}_{n}(t)\ \forall t\in[0,T]
.
Following from the inequality in display (
E.10
),

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}(\tilde{\xi}_{n},\tilde{\xi}^{\prime}_{n})

\displaystyle<\frac{\Delta}{2}+\int_{T}^{\infty}e^{-t}\cdot\Big[\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}\Big(\phi_{t}\big(\tilde{\xi}_{n}),\ \phi_{t}\big(\tilde{\xi}^{\prime}_{n}\big)\Big)\wedge 1\Big]dt

\displaystyle<\Delta\qquad\text{by \eqref{proof: choice of T, lemma: bounded away condition under J1, from infty to T}},

which clearly contradicts
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(B,\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{x}}[0,\infty)\big)>\Delta.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Theorem
E.3
,
which adapts Theorem
3.2
to
\big(\mathbb{D}[0,\infty),\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}\big)
.

Theorem E.3
.

Let Assumption
5
hold.
Let

\bm{k}=(k_{j})_{j\in[d]}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{d}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
,
and let
B
be a Borel set of
\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)
equipped with
J_{1}
topology (i.e., induced by
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}
).
Suppose that
B
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)
under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}
for some
\epsilon>0
.
Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}(B^{\circ})\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}\in B\big)}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}\in B\big)}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}\leq\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}(B^{-})<\infty,

(E.11)

where

{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}
,

\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}
,

\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}
,
and
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)

are defined in
(
3.12
),
(
3.4
),
(
3.10
),
and (
3.17
),
respectively.

Proof.

Part
(ii)
of Lemma
E.1
confirms that

\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}\in\mathbb{M}\big(\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)\setminus\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)\big)

(under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}
) for any
\epsilon>0
,
which verifies the finite upper bound in (
E.11
).
Then,
by Theorem
A.2
(the Portmanteau Theorem for
\mathbb{M}
-convergence),
it suffices to prove the following claim:
for any
f:\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)

uniformly continuous
(w.r.t.
(\mathbb{D}[0,\infty),\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}})
) and bounded (i.e.,
\left\lVert f\right\rVert=\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)}f(\xi)<\infty
)
such that

B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\text{supp}(f)
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)

under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}
for some
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}f(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}})}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}=\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}(f).

(E.12)

To proceed, for each
t\in[0,\infty)
we define the mapping
f_{t}:\mathbb{D}[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}
by
(note that in the display (
E.13
) below, the path
\xi
is an element of
\mathbb{D}[0,t]
)

\displaystyle f_{t}(\xi)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}f\big(\phi^{\text{inv}}_{t}(\xi)\big),\quad\text{where }\phi^{\text{inv}}_{t}(\xi)(s)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\xi(s\wedge t)+[0\vee(s-t)]\cdot\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}\ \forall s\geq 0.

(E.13)

That is,
\phi^{\text{inv}}_{t}
extends the path
\xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,t]
to
\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)
linearly with slope
\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}
over
(t,\infty)
, which, in a way, “inverts” the projection mapping
\phi_{t}
in (
3.23
).
Now, we make a few observations.

•

Define
\hat{\phi}_{t}:\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)
by

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}_{t}(\xi)(s)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\xi(t\wedge s)+[0\vee(s-t)]\cdot\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}},\qquad\forall\xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty),\ s\geq 0.

By the definition of
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}
in (
E.1
),
for any
t>0
and
\xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)
, we have
