\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}\big(\hat{\phi}_{t}(\xi),\xi\big)\leq\int_{s>t}e^{-s}ds=e^{-t}.

Then, by the uniform continuity of the function
f
fixed in (
E.12
), given
\epsilon>0
, there exists
T=T(\epsilon)>0
such that

\displaystyle\big|f(\xi)-f\big(\hat{\phi}_{t}(\xi)\big)\big|<\epsilon,\qquad\forall t\geq T,\ \xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty).

(E.14)

•

Recall that
B=\text{supp}(f)
, and note that

\displaystyle f(\xi)=0\text{ and }f\big(\hat{\phi}_{t}(\xi)\big)=0,\qquad\forall t>0,\ \xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)\text{ such that }\phi_{t}(\xi)\notin\phi_{t}(B).

(E.15)

•

Combining (
E.14
) and (
E.15
),
we know that given
\epsilon>0
, there is
T=T(\epsilon)\in(0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\big|f(\xi)-f\big(\hat{\phi}_{t}(\xi)\big)\big|\leq\epsilon\cdot\mathbbm{I}\big\{\phi_{t}(\xi)\in\phi_{t}(B)\big\},\qquad\forall t\geq T,\ \xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty).

(E.16)

•

Recall that in (
E.13
), we use
\xi
to denote an element of
\mathbb{D}[0,t]
, which differs from our convention henceforth that
\xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)
.
By definitions in (
E.13
),
it holds for any
t>0
and
\xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)
that

f_{t}\big(\phi_{t}(\xi)\big)=f\big(\hat{\phi}_{t}(\xi)\big).

Then, by (
E.16
), given
\epsilon>0
there exists
T=T(\epsilon)>0
such that
for any
t\geq T
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}f(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}})}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}f_{t}(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,t]}})}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}+\epsilon\cdot\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,t]}}\in\phi_{t}(B))}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)},

(E.17)

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}f(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}})}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}f_{t}(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,t]}})}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}-\epsilon\cdot\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,t]}}\in\phi_{t}(B))}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}.

(E.18)

Suppose we can show that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}f_{t}(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,t]}})}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}=\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}(f),

(E.19)

\displaystyle\limsup_{t\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,t]}}\in\phi_{t}(B))}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}<\infty.

(E.20)

Then, by plugging these results into (
E.17
)–(
E.18
) and sending
t\to\infty
,
\epsilon\downarrow 0
,
we conclude the proof of claim (
E.12
).
Now, it only remains to prove claims (
E.19
) and (
E.20
).

Proof of Claim (
E.19
)
.
For any
t>0
, the function
f_{t}
is continuous and bounded.
Besides, note that

f_{t}(\xi)=0
whenever
\xi\notin\phi_{t}(B)
,
where
B=\text{supp}(f)
.
On the other hand,
Lemma
E.2
shows that
\phi_{t}(B)
is bounded away from

\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,t]
under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}
for any
t>0
large enough.
This allows us to apply Theorem
3.2
and Theorem
A.2
, the Portmanteau Theorem for
\mathbb{M}
-convergence, to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{E}f_{t}(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,t]}})}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}=\sum_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})}\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}(f_{t}),\qquad\forall t>0\text{ sufficiently large.}

Also, note that
|\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})|<\infty
(i.e., the set contains only finitely many elements).
Therefore, to prove Claim (
E.19
),
it suffices to fix some
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\in\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})

and show that

\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}(f_{t})=\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}(f)

for any
t>0
sufficiently large.
Now,
by the definition of
f_{t}
in (
E.13
) and

\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}

in (
3.10
),

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}(f_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}!}\cdot\int f\Bigg(\phi^{\text{inv}}_{t}\bigg(\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}\bm{1}+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},t]}\bigg)\Bigg)

(E.21)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bigg((\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\times\mathcal{L}_{(0,t)})\Big(d(\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k},t_{\bm{j},k})\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}!}\cdot\int f\Bigg(\phi^{\text{inv}}_{t}\bigg(\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}\bm{1}+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},t]}\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\mathbbm{I}\Big\{t_{\bm{j},k}<t\ \forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]\Big\}\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bigg((\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\times\mathcal{L}_{(0,\infty)})\Big(d(\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k},t_{\bm{j},k})\Big)\bigg),

where
\mathcal{L}_{I}
is the Lebesgue measure restricted on interval
I
.
Furthermore, for
B=\text{supp}(f)
, by part
(i)
of Lemma
E.1
,
there exists some
T\in(0,\infty)
such that the following claim holds:
for any piece-wise linear function
\xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)
of the form

\xi(t)=\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}t+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},\infty)}(t)

with
\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j})
for each
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]
,
we have

\displaystyle\xi\in B\quad\Longrightarrow\quad t_{\bm{j},k}<T\ \forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}].

(E.22)

Therefore,
provided that
\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j})
for each
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
and
k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]
,
given any
t>T
we have

\displaystyle f\Bigg(\phi^{\text{inv}}_{t}\bigg(\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}\bm{1}+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},t]}\bigg)\Bigg)\cdot\mathbbm{I}\Big\{t_{\bm{j},k}<t\ \forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]\Big\}

\displaystyle=f\Bigg(\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}\bm{1}+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},\infty)}\Bigg)\cdot\mathbbm{I}\Big\{t_{\bm{j},k}<t\ \forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]\Big\}\quad\text{by \eqref{proof, def phi t inverse, corollary: LD for Levy with MRV},}

\displaystyle=f\Bigg(\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}\bm{1}+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},\infty)}\Bigg)\qquad\text{due to \eqref{proof, intermediate goal 1, choice of T, corollary: LD for Levy with MRV} and $B=\text{supp}(f)$}.

Also,
by the
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition of Assumption
5
,
for each
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}
the measure
\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}(\cdot)
is supported on
\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j})
(see also Section
2.1
).
Then, in (
E.21
), it holds for any
t>T
that

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}(f_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}!}\cdot\int f\Bigg(\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}\bm{1}+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},\infty)}\Bigg)\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\bigtimes_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bigg((\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\times\mathcal{L}_{(0,\infty)})\Big(d(\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k},t_{\bm{j},k})\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}(f).

This concludes the proof of Claim (
E.19
).

Proof of Claim (
E.20
)
.
By the definition of the projection mapping
\phi_{t}
in (
3.23
),
for any
t^{\prime}>t>0
we have

\big\{\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,t]}}\in\phi_{t}(B)\big\}\supseteq\big\{\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,t^{\prime}]}}\in\phi_{t^{\prime}}(B)\big\}.

This monotonicity w.r.t.
t

implies that, to prove Claim (
E.20
),
it suffices to find
T\in(0,\infty)
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,T]}}\in\phi_{T}(B))}{\breve{\lambda}_{\bm{k}}(n)}

\displaystyle<\infty.

(E.23)

Since
B=\text{supp}(f)
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,\infty)

under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}
for some
\epsilon>0
,
Lemma
E.2
shows that
\phi_{T}(B)
is bounded away from

\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{k};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,T]
under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}
for any
T>0
large enough.
Claim (
E.23
) then follows from the finite upper bound in Claim (
3.19
) of Theorem
3.2
.
∎

E.2
Proof of Proposition
3.4

To rigorously explain the coupling between the Hawkes process
\bm{N}(t)
and the compound Poisson process
\bm{L}(t)
in (
3.32
),
we first review the cluster representation of Hawkes processes,
which reveals the underlying branching structure in
\bm{N}(t)
.
Intuitively speaking, the cluster representation shows that the Hawkes process
\bm{N}(t)
can be constructed by first generating a sequence of “immigrants” (i.e., points of the
0^{\text{th}}
generation) arriving at Poisson rates
c^{\bm{N}}_{i}

and then, iteratively, letting each generation of points to give birth to the next generation of descendants.
In particular, any type-
j
point gives birth to type-
i
points
according to an inhomogeneous Poisson processes with rates determined by

\tilde{B}_{i\leftarrow j}f^{\bm{N}}_{i\leftarrow j}(\cdot).

This approach was first introduced in
[
42
]
in the univariate setting. See also Chapter 4 of
[
54
]
and Examples 6.3(c) and 6.4(c) of
[
26
]
for a treatment in the multivariate setting.

Appealing to the more general framework of Poisson cluster processes,
we start by constructing a point process that identifies the
centers
of each
cluster, and then augment each center with a separate point process representing the arrival times and types of the offspring in this cluster.
First, we define a point process in the space
\bm{N}^{\mathcal{C}}(\cdot)
in
[0,\infty)\times[d]
, where the superscript
\mathcal{C}
denotes “center”.
Recall the positive immigration rates
c^{\bm{N}}_{i}
in (
1.1
).
Independently for each
j\in[d]
, generate

0<T^{\mathcal{C}}_{i;1}<T^{\mathcal{C}}_{i;2}<\ldots

under a Poisson process with rate
c^{\bm{N}}_{i}
.
Let

\displaystyle\bm{N}^{\mathcal{C}}(\cdot)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{\delta}_{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)}(\cdot),

(E.24)

where
\bm{\delta}_{x}
denotes the Dirac measure at
x
.
This is equivalent to the superposition of several independent Poisson processes, where each arrival
T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}
is augmented with a marker
j
to denote its type.

Next, we associate each point
(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)
in the center process with an
offspring
process, which is a point process in the space
[0,\infty)\times[d]

(we use the superscript
\mathcal{O}
to denote “offspring”):

\displaystyle\bm{N}^{\mathcal{O}}_{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)}(\cdot)=\sum_{m=0}^{K^{\mathcal{O}}{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)}}\bm{\delta}_{(T^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m),A^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m))}(\cdot).

(E.25)

In particular, each
\bm{N}^{\mathcal{O}}_{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)}(\cdot)
is an independent copy of a point process
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
, whose law is described by the following iterative procedure.

Definition E.4

(Offspring Cluster Process
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
)
.

For each
j\in[d]
, the point process
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}(\cdot)
in the space
[0,\infty)\times[d]
is defined as follows.

1.

(
Ancestor Immigrant
)
In this procedure, we use
R^{(n);j}_{i}
to denote the number of type-
i
individuals in the
n^{\text{th}}
generation of the cluster
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
,
and
T^{(n);j}_{i}(k)
for the time between the arrival of the ancestor of the cluster and the birth of the
k^{\text{th}}
type-
i
individual in the
n^{\text{th}}
generation.
Specifically,
let
R^{(0);j}_{j}=1
and
R^{(0);j}_{i}=0
for each
i\in[d],\ i\neq j
,
which represents that the type-
j
ancestor is the only member in the
0^{\text{th}}
generation of this cluster.
Besides,
we set
T^{(0);j}_{j}(1)=0
.

2.

(
Offspring in the
n^{\text{th}}
generation
)
Iteratively,
do the following for
n=1,2,\ldots
,
until we arrive at some
n
such that
R^{(n-1);j}_{i}=0
for any
i\in[d]
.
For any
l\in[d]
with
R^{(n-1);j}_{l}\geq 1
, and any
m\in[R^{(n-1);j}_{l}]
,
independently for each
i\in[d]
, generate the sequence

\big(T^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow l}(k)\big)_{k}

according to an inhomogeneous Poisson process with rate

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{i\leftarrow l}f^{\bm{N}}_{i\leftarrow l}\big(\ \bm{\cdot}\ -T^{(n-1);j}_{l}(m)\big).

(E.26)

Here, by sampling under the rate in (
E.26
), we mean the following.

•

First, generate an independent copy of
\tilde{B}_{i\leftarrow l}
.
Conditioning on
\tilde{B}_{i\leftarrow l}=b
for some
b\geq 0
,
generate
B^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow l}
—the count of type-
i
children (in the
n^{\text{th}}
generation) born by the
m^{\text{th}}
type-
l
individual in the
(n-1)^{\text{th}}
generation—by

B^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow l}\sim\text{Poisson}\big(b\cdot\mu^{\bm{N}}_{i\leftarrow l}\big),

with
\mu^{\bm{N}}_{i\leftarrow l}
defined in (
2.8
).

•

Next, let
t_{k}
be i.i.d. copies under the law with density
f^{\bm{N}}_{i\leftarrow l}(\cdot)/\mu^{\bm{N}}_{i\leftarrow l}
.
Let
T^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow l}(k)=t_{k}+T^{(n-1);j}_{l}(m)
for each

k\in[B^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow j}]
.

Then, given
i\in[d]
, by
ordering the arrival times
T^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow l}(k)

across all
l\in[d]
,
m\in[R^{(n-1);j}_{l}]
, and
k\in[B^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow l}]
,
we get

0<T^{(n);j}_{i}(1)<T^{(n);j}_{i}(2)<\ldots<T^{(n);j}_{i}\big(R^{(n);j}_{i}\big),

where we use
R^{(n);j}_{i}
to denote the count of type-
i
individuals in the
n^{\text{th}}
generation.
Note that this strictly increasing sequence is almost surely well-defined (i.e., with no ties)
since the law of
T^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow l}(k)

is absolutely continuous w.r.t. the Lebesgue measure.

3.

(
Definition of the cluster process
)
Let
K^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.19441pt\raisebox{5.64096pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-3.19441pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\max\{n\geq 0:\ R^{(n);j}_{i}\geq 1\text{ for some }i\in[d]\}

be the count of generations in this cluster.
W.l.o.g. we set
R^{(n);j}_{i}=0
for any
n\geq K^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}+1
and
i\in[d]
.
The cluster process is defined by

\displaystyle C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}(\cdot)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.19441pt\raisebox{5.64096pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-3.19441pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{n=0}^{K^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{m=1}^{R^{(n);j}_{i}}\bm{\delta}_{(T^{(n);j}_{i}(m),i)}(\cdot).

(E.27)

In this paper, we work with conditions (see Section
2.2
) which ensure that there are almost surely finitely many points in
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
,
and hence the count of points
K^{\mathcal{O}}{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)}
is almost surely finite in (
E.25
).

Define
the time-shift operator
\bm{\theta}_{t}
by

\bm{\theta}_{t}\mu(\cdot)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{k\geq 1}\bm{\delta}_{(t_{k}+t,a_{k})}(\cdot)

for any
t\geq 0
and point process
\mu(\cdot)=\sum_{k\geq 1}\bm{\delta}_{(t_{k},a_{k})}
.
Augmenting the center process
\bm{N}^{\mathcal{C}}
with each offspring cluster
\bm{N}^{\mathcal{O}}_{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)}
,
we obtain a point process

\displaystyle\bm{N}(\cdot)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{\theta}_{T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}}\bm{N}^{\mathcal{O}}_{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)}(\cdot)=\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{m=0}^{K^{\mathcal{O}}{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)}}\bm{\delta}_{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}+T^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m),A^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m))}(\cdot).

(E.28)

For each
t\geq 0
and
i\in[d]
, we define the counting process

\displaystyle N_{i}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bm{N}\big([0,t]\times\{i\}\big)=\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{m=0}^{K^{\mathcal{O}}{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)}}\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}+T^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m)\leq t,\ A^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m)=i\bigg\}.

(E.29)

Under the sub-criticality condition regarding the offspring distributions (i.e., Assumption
1
),
it has been shown in
[
42
,
26
]
that the definitions in
(
E.24
)–(
E.29
)
using the cluster representation agree with Definition
1.1
.

We formally define the size of the offspring cluster processes
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
.
Using notations in Definition
E.4
,
we define (for each
j\in[d]
)

\displaystyle S_{i\leftarrow j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{n\geq 0}R^{(n);j}_{i},\qquad\bm{S}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}(S_{1\leftarrow j},S_{2\leftarrow j},\ldots,S_{d\leftarrow j})^{\top}.

(E.30)

That is,

\bm{S}_{j}
is the size vector of the offspring cluster process
\mathcal{C}^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}(\cdot)
induced by a type-
j
ancestor,
with each element
S_{i\leftarrow j}
representing the count of type-
i
points in the cluster.
Under the law of
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}(\cdot)
specified above,
the vectors
\bm{S}_{j}
solve the distributional fixed-point equations (
1.2
),
under the offspring distributions
(B_{i\leftarrow j})_{i\in[d]}
stated in (
2.7
).

Meanwhile,
using notations in (
E.25
),
we denote the size vector of cluster
\bm{N}^{\mathcal{O}}_{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},j)}
by

\displaystyle S^{(k)}_{i\leftarrow j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{m\geq 0}\mathbbm{I}\Big\{A^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m)=i\Big\},\qquad\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big(S^{(k)}_{1\leftarrow j},S^{(k)}_{2\leftarrow j},\ldots,S^{(k)}_{d\leftarrow j}\Big)^{\top}.

(E.31)

By definition,
\big(\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\big)_{k\geq 1}
are i.i.d. copies of the
\bm{S}_{j}
defined in (
E.30
),
and hence exhibit the
\mathcal{MHRV}^{*}
tail asymptotics characterized in Theorem
2.2
.

In the construction of
\bm{L}(t)
in (
3.32
),
let
\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}
be defined as in (
E.31
).
Now,
Proposition
3.4
is an immediate consequence of the construction of the compound Poisson process
\bm{L}(t)
in (
3.32
) and the tail asymptotics of Hawkes process clusters established in Theorem
2.2
.

Proof of Proposition
3.4
.

Note that
\bm{L}(t)
is the superposition of a sequence of independent compound Poisson processes.
That is,
\bm{L}(t)=\sum_{j\in[d]}\bm{L}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(t)
, where

\displaystyle\bm{L}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\mathbbm{I}_{[T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},\infty)}(t),\qquad\forall j\in[d],

the
(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k})_{k\geq 1}
is a sequence generated by a Poisson process on
(0,\infty)
with a constant rate
c_{j}^{\bm{N}}
, and
(\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j})_{k\geq 1}
are i.i.d. copies of
\bm{S}_{j}
.
In other words,

\bm{L}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(t)
is a Lévy process with generating triplet
(\bm{0},\textbf{0},\nu_{j})
,
where the Lévy measure is

\nu_{j}(\cdot)=c^{\bm{N}}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{P}(\bm{S}_{j}\in\ \cdot\ ).

Therefore, Claim (
3.33
) follows from
\bm{L}(t)=\sum_{j\in[d]}\bm{L}_{\bm{\cdot}\leftarrow j}(t)
.
Next, by Theorem
2.2
,

\displaystyle\nu_{i}\in\mathcal{MHRV}^{*}\Bigg((\bar{\bm{s}}_{j})_{j\in[d]},\ \big(\alpha(\bm{j})\big)_{\bm{j}\subseteq[d]},\ (\lambda_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}},\ \Big(c_{i}^{\bm{N}}\cdot\mathbf{C}^{\bm{j}}_{i}\Big)_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{d}}\Bigg),\qquad\forall i\in[d].

Then, the
\mathcal{MHRV}^{*}
tail condition of
\nu=\sum_{i\in[d]}\nu_{i}
claimed in (
3.34
)
follows from Definition
2.1
for
\mathcal{MHRV}^{*}
,
as well as the definition of

\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}=\sum_{i\in[d]}c^{\bm{N}}_{i}\cdot\mathbf{C}^{\bm{j}}_{i}
in (
3.25
).
∎

E.3
Proof of Proposition
3.5

To prove Proposition
3.5
, we prepare a few lemmas.
First, we decompose
\bm{L}(t)
in (
3.32
) into processes constructed by “large” clusters and “small” clusters separately.
For each
\delta,T>0
and
n\geq 1
, let

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\right\rVert>n\delta\bigg\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}_{[T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},\infty)}(nt),

(E.32)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)-\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\right\rVert\leq n\delta\bigg\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}_{[T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},\infty)}(nt).

(E.33)

We also define the (scaled) sample paths

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,T]}}_{n}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t):\ t\in[0,T]\big\},\qquad\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t):\ t\geq 0\big\},

(E.34)

and adopt notations
\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta;_{[0,T]}}_{n}
and
\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}
analogously.
Recall that

\mathbf{1}(t)=t
is the linear function with slope
1
, and that

\mathbf{E}\bm{L}(1)=\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}
(see (
3.27
)).
The next lemma establishes the asymptotic equivalence between
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}
and
\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}+\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}\mathbf{1}
under
\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}
.

Lemma E.5
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
For any
\Delta,\beta>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\beta}\cdot\mathbf{P}\bigg(\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\Big(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}},\ \bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}+\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}\mathbf{1}\Big)>\Delta\bigg)=0,\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough.}

Proof.

Fix
T>0
large enough such that
e^{-T}<\Delta/2
.
Note that

\displaystyle\int_{t>T}e^{-t}\cdot\Big[\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{[0,t]}}\Big(\phi_{t}\big(\xi^{(1)}),\ \phi_{t}\big(\xi^{(2)}\big)\Big)\wedge 1\Big]dt\leq e^{-T}<\Delta/2,\qquad\forall\xi^{(1)},\xi^{(2)}\in\mathbb{D}[0,\infty).

Besides , for each
t\in[0,T]
, the metric
\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,t]}}}
is bounded by the uniform metric on
\mathbb{D}[0,t]
.
Therefore,
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)-\Big(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{>\delta}(t)+\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\Big)\right\rVert>\Delta/2\bigg)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough.}

(E.35)

To proceed, let (for each
k\geq 1
)

\displaystyle\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{t>\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k-1):\ \Delta\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}(t)\neq 0\big\}=\big\{t>\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k-1):\ \left\lVert\Delta\bar{\bm{L}}(t)\right\rVert>\delta\big\}

(E.36)

be the arrival time of the
k^{\text{th}}
large jump in
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)
,
under the convention that
\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(0)=0
.
Meanwhile,
for any non-negative integer
K
,
on the event
\big\{\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(K+1)>T\big\}
(meaning that there are at most
K
large jumps in
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)
during
t\in[0,T]
),
note that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)-\Big(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{>\delta}(t)+\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\Big)\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}(t)-\mu_{\bm{N}}t\right\rVert\quad\text{see \eqref{def: small jump process for Levy, LD for Hawkes}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K+1}\underbrace{\sup_{t\in[\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k-1),\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k))\cap[0,T]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}\cdot\big(t-\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k-1)\big)-\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}\big(\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k-1)\big)\right\rVert}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}R_{k}(\delta)}.

In the last line of the display above, we applied
\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(K+1)>T
,
as well as the fact that
\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)
makes no jumps at any
t=\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k)
; see the definitions in (
E.33
).
Therefore, to prove Claim (
E.35
),
it suffices to show the existence of some
K\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(K+1)\leq T\Big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ sufficiently small},

(E.37)

and that for each
k\in[K+1]
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(R_{k}(\delta)>\frac{\Delta}{2(K+1)}\Bigg)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ sufficiently small}.

(E.38)

Proof of Claim (
E.37
)
.
We prove that, for
K
large enough, the claim holds for any
\delta>0
.
In light of Proposition
3.4
and the definitions in (
E.32
)–(
E.33
),

\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}(t)
is a Lévy process with generating triplet
\Big(\bm{0},\textbf{0},n\cdot\nu_{n}\big(\ \cdot\ \cap\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\delta\}\big)\Big)
,
where
\nu_{n}(A)=\nu\{n\bm{x}:\ \bm{x}\in A\}
, and
\nu
is defined in (
3.33
), satisfying the
\mathcal{MHRV}^{*}
tail condition in (
3.34
).
Next, let
\alpha^{*}(\cdot)
and
\alpha(\cdot)
be defined as in (
2.11
)–(
2.12
),
and let
j^{*}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\underset{j\in[d]}{\arg\min}\ \bm{c}\big(\{j\}\big)=\underset{j\in[d]}{\arg\min}\ \alpha^{*}(j).

Note that this argument minimum is unique and
\alpha^{*}(j^{*})>1
under Assumptions
2
and
4
.
As a result,
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\{j^{*}\},\epsilon)=\{\bm{0}\}
for any
\epsilon>0
.
By (
2.5
),

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{n\nu_{n}\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\delta\}}{n\lambda_{\{j^{*}\}}(n)}<\infty,\qquad\forall\delta>0,

(E.39)

where
\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)
is defined in (
2.13
).
This implies

n\nu_{n}\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\delta\}=\mathcal{O}\big(n\lambda_{\{j^{*}\}}(n)\big)

as
n\to\infty
.
Then, for any
K\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(K+1)\leq T\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(\text{Poisson}\big(n\nu_{n}\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\delta\}\cdot T\big)\geq K+1\Big)

\displaystyle\leq T^{K+1}\cdot\big(n\nu_{n}\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\delta\}\big)^{K+1}\quad\text{due to }\mathbf{P}\big(\text{Poisson}(\lambda)\geq k\big)\leq\lambda^{k}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Big(\big(n\lambda_{\{j^{*}\}}(n)\big)^{K+1}\Big)\qquad\text{by \eqref{proof, bound 1, sub goal 1, lemma: asymptotic equivalence between L and large jump, LD for Hawkes}.}

Lastly, due to
n\lambda_{\{j^{*}\}}(n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-(\alpha^{*}(j^{*})-1)}(n)
(see (
2.13
)) and
\alpha^{*}(j^{*})>1
,
it holds for all
K
large enough that
(K+1)\cdot\big(\alpha^{*}(j^{*})-1\big)>\beta
, and hence

\big(n\lambda_{\{j^{*}\}}(n)\big)^{K+1}=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}).

This concludes the proof of Claim (
E.37
).

Proof of Claim (
E.38
)
.
By the Markov property at each stopping time
\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k)
,
it suffices to show that (for any
\delta>0
small enough),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,T]:\ t<\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(1)}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\right\rVert>\frac{\Delta}{2(K+1)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,T]:\ t<\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(1)}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\right\rVert>\frac{\Delta}{2(K+1)}\Bigg)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),

where we applied the fact that
\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)=\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)
for any
t<\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(1)
; see (
E.32
)–(
E.33
).
Besides, recall that
\mathbf{E}\bm{L}(1)=\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}
.
Applying Lemma
D.4
, we conclude the proof of claim (
E.38
).
∎

Analogous to Lemma
E.5
, we decompose the Hawkes process
\bm{N}(t)
by considering the small or large clusters therein.
More precisely,
for the scaled process
\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}(t)=\bm{N}(nt)/n
,
it follows from the cluster representation (
E.29
) that

\displaystyle\bar{N}_{n,i}(t)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{m=0}^{K^{\mathcal{O}}{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j}(k),j)}}\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}+T^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m)\leq nt,\ A^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m)=i\bigg\},\quad\forall t\geq 0,\ i\in[d],

and
\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}(t)=\big(\bar{N}_{n,1}(t),\bar{N}_{n,2}(t),\ldots,\bar{N}_{n,d}(t)\big)^{\top}
.
Meanwhile, recall that
\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}
is size vector of the cluster induced by the
k^{\text{th}}
type-
j
immigrant (see (
E.31
))
and
T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}
is the arrival time of that immigrant.
By incorporating the cluster size, for each
n\geq 1
and
\delta>0
we define

\displaystyle\bar{N}_{n,i}^{>\delta}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{m=0}^{K^{\mathcal{O}}{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j}(k),j)}}\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\right\rVert>n\delta\bigg\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}+T^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m)\leq nt,\ A^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m)=i\bigg\},

(E.40)

\displaystyle\bar{N}_{n,i}^{\leqslant\delta}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bar{N}_{n,i}(t)-\bar{N}_{n,i}^{>\delta}(t)

(E.41)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\sum_{m=0}^{K^{\mathcal{O}}{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j}(k),j)}}\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\right\rVert\leq n\delta\bigg\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}+T^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m)\leq nt,\ A^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m)=i\bigg\},

and

\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big(\bar{N}_{n,1}^{>\delta}(t),\ldots,\bar{N}_{n,d}^{>\delta}(t)\big)^{\top},

\bar{\bm{N}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big(\bar{N}_{n,1}^{\leqslant\delta}(t),\ldots,\bar{N}_{n,d}^{\leqslant\delta}(t)\big)^{\top}.

Analogous to (
E.34
), we define

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,T]}}_{n}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t):\ t\in[0,T]\big\},\qquad\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\big\{\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t):\ t\geq 0\big\}.

(E.42)

The proof of the next lemma involves the lifetime of each cluster.
Adopting notations in (
E.25
), we use

\displaystyle H^{(k)}_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\max_{m\geq 0}T^{\mathcal{O}}_{j;k}(m)

(E.43)

to denote the lifetime of the cluster process
\bm{N}^{\mathcal{O}}_{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j}(k),j)}

(i.e., the gap in the birth times between the ancestor and the last descendant in this cluster).
Similarly, in (
E.27
) we use

\displaystyle H_{j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\max_{m\geq 0,\ i\in[d]}T^{(n);j}_{i}(m)

(E.44)

to denote the lifetime of the point process
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
.
Since

\bm{N}^{\mathcal{O}}_{(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j}(k),j)}

are i.i.d. copies of
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
,
the sequence
H^{(k)}_{j}
are also independent copies of
H_{j}
.
Lemma
E.6
establishes the asymptotic equivalence between
\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}
and
\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}+\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}\mathbf{1}
under
\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}
.

Lemma E.6
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
For any
\Delta,\beta>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}n^{\beta}\cdot\mathbf{P}\bigg(\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\Big(\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}},\ \bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}+\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}\mathbf{1}\Big)>\Delta\bigg)=0,\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough.}

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
E.5
.
In particular, by fixing
T
large enough such that
e^{-T}<\Delta/2
, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}(t)-\Big(\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}^{>\delta}(t)+\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\Big)\right\rVert>\Delta/2\bigg)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta}),\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough.}

(E.45)

First, by comparing the definitions in (
E.33
) and (
E.41
),

\displaystyle\bm{\bar{N}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)\leq\bm{\bar{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t),\qquad\forall\delta>0,\ n\geq 1,\ t\geq 0,

(E.46)

since in
\bm{\bar{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)
we lump all descendants in the same cluster at the arrival time of the immigrant of the cluster.
Here, the order
\bm{x}\leq\bm{y}
between two vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
means that
x_{i}\leq y_{i}
for each
i\in[d]
.
Next, for each
M>0
, let

\displaystyle{\bm{L}}^{|M}(t)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\mathbbm{I}\Big\{H^{(k)}_{j}\leq M\Big\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}_{[T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},\infty)}(t).

That is,
{\bm{L}}^{|M}(t)
is a modification of
\bm{L}(t)
in (
3.32
) by only considering clusters with lifetime below
M
.
Note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\big[\bm{L}^{|M}(1)\big]=\sum_{j\in[d]}c_{j}^{\bm{N}}\cdot\mathbf{E}\big[\bm{S}_{j}\mathbbm{I}\{H_{j}\leq M\}\big]\rightarrow\sum_{j\in[d]}c_{j}^{\bm{N}}\cdot\mathbf{E}\bm{S}_{j}=\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}},\qquad\text{as }M\to\infty,

(E.47)

by the monotone convergence theorem.
Besides, for each
n\geq 1
and
\delta>0
, we define processes

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{L}}^{|M}_{n}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\mathbbm{I}\big\{H^{(k)}_{j}\leq M\big\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}_{[T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},\infty)}(nt),

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta|M}_{n}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{\left\lVert\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}\right\rVert\leqslant n\delta,\ H^{(k)}_{j}\leq M\bigg\}\cdot\mathbbm{I}_{[T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k},\infty)}(nt),

which scale both time and space by
n
.
For any
\epsilon,\delta>0
, and any
n
large enough such that
n\epsilon>M
,

\displaystyle\bm{\bar{N}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)\geq\bm{\bar{L}}^{\leqslant\delta|M}_{n}\big((t-\epsilon)\vee 0\big),\qquad\forall\delta>0,\ n\geq 1,\ t\geq 0.

(E.48)

Indeed, for any cluster with its immigrant arriving at
T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}\leq n(t-\epsilon)
and lifetime
H^{(k)}_{j}\leq M
,
all the descendants in this cluster must have arrived by the time
n(t-\epsilon)+M<nt
.
Combining (
E.46
) and (
E.48
),
for any
\epsilon,M,\delta>0
, it holds for all
n
large enough with
n\epsilon>M
that

\displaystyle\bm{\bar{L}}^{\leqslant\delta|M}_{n}\big((t-\epsilon)\vee 0\big)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\leq\bm{\bar{N}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\leq\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t,\qquad\forall t\geq 0

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bm{\bar{N}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\right\rVert\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\right\rVert+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bm{\bar{L}}^{\leqslant\delta|M}_{n}\big((t-\epsilon)\vee 0\big)-\mu_{\bm{N}}t\right\rVert.

The last step follows from our choice of the
L_{1}
norm and the preliminary bound that

y\leq x\leq z\ \Longrightarrow\ |x|\leq|y|+|z|.

Furthermore, note that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bm{\bar{L}}^{\leqslant\delta|M}_{n}\big((t-\epsilon)\vee 0\big)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bm{\bar{L}}^{\leqslant\delta|M}_{n}(t)-t\cdot\mathbf{E}\big[\bm{L}^{|M}(1)\big]\right\rVert+\epsilon\left\lVert\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}\right\rVert+T\cdot\left\lVert\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}-\mathbf{E}\big[\bm{L}^{|M}(1)\big]\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bm{\bar{L}}^{\leqslant\delta|M}_{n}(t)-t\cdot\mathbf{E}\big[\bm{L}^{|M}(1)\big]\right\rVert+\Delta/6\qquad\text{for any $\epsilon$ small enough and $M$ large enough}.

The last inequality follows from the limit in (
E.47
).
Therefore, to prove Claim (
E.45
),
it suffices to show that given
M>0
,
the bounds

\mathbf{P}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bm{\bar{L}}^{\leqslant\delta|M}_{n}(t)-t\cdot\mathbf{E}\big[\bm{L}^{|M}(1)\big]\right\rVert>\Delta/6\Big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta})

and

\mathbf{P}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\lVert\bm{\bar{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}(t)-\bm{\mu}_{\bm{N}}t\right\rVert>\Delta/6\Big)=\mathit{o}(n^{-\beta})

for any
\delta>0
small enough.
By repeating the arguments in Lemma
E.5
for (
E.35
), one can establish both claims above.
∎

To proceed, we prepare
Lemma
E.7
and characterize the tail asymptotics for
H_{j}
in (
E.44
) (i.e., the lifetime of clusters).

Lemma E.7
.

Let Assumptions
1
and
2
hold.
Let
\beta>0

and
a:(0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
be a regularly varying function with
a(x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\beta}(x)
as
x\to\infty
.
Let
f^{\bm{N}}_{p\leftarrow q}(\cdot)
be the decay functions in (
1.1
).
Suppose that

\displaystyle\int_{x/\log x}^{\infty}f^{\bm{N}}_{p\leftarrow q}(t)dt=\mathit{o}\big(a(x)\big)\ \text{ as }x\to\infty,\qquad\forall p,q\in[d].

(E.49)

Then,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(H_{j}>n\epsilon)=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big)\ \text{ as }n\to\infty,\qquad\forall j\in[d],\ \epsilon>0.

(E.50)

Proof.

In this proof, we fix some
\epsilon>0
and
j\in[d]
.
Recall the cluster representation of Hawkes processes in (
E.24
)–(
E.29
).
The key of this proof is to establish a stochastic comparison of
H_{j}
, the lifetime of a cluster
\mathcal{C}^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
,
which refines the bounds for the lifetime of clusters in
[
61
]
by taking the maximum instead of the sum of the birth times within each generation.
To be more specific, we introduce a few notations.
Independently for each pair
(i,l)\in[d]^{2}
,
let
\big(B^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow l}\big)_{n,m\geq 1}

be
the i.i.d. copies of
B_{i\leftarrow l}
in (
2.7
), i.e., with law

\displaystyle B_{i\leftarrow l}\sim\text{Poisson}\bigg(\tilde{B}_{i\leftarrow l}\int_{0}^{\infty}f^{\bm{N}}_{i\leftarrow l}(t)dt\bigg).

Here, recall that
by Poisson
(X)
for a non-negative variable
X
, we mean the law

\mathbf{P}\big(\text{Poisson}(X)>y\big)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(\text{Poisson}(x)>y\big)\mathbf{P}(X\in dx)
.
This agrees with the notations in Definition
E.4
,
where we use
B^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow l}
to denote, in the cluster process
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}(\cdot)
,
the count of type-
i
children (in the
n^{\text{th}}
generation) born by the
m^{\text{th}}
type-
l
individual in the
(n-1)^{\text{th}}
generation.
Besides, independent from the sequence
\big(B^{(n,m);j}_{i\leftarrow l}\big)_{n,m\geq 1}
,
let
\big(X_{i\leftarrow l}^{(n,k,k^{\prime})}\big)_{n,k,k^{\prime}\geq 1}

be i.i.d. copies of
X_{i\leftarrow l}
with law

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(X_{i\leftarrow l}\in E)=\frac{\int_{t\in E}f^{\bm{N}}_{i\leftarrow l}(t)dt}{\int_{0}^{\infty}f^{\bm{N}}_{i\leftarrow l}(t)dt},\qquad\forall\text{ Borel }E\subseteq(0,\infty).

(E.51)

By step (ii) in Definition
E.4
,
we interpret

X_{i\leftarrow l}^{(n,k,k^{\prime})}
as the time that the
k^{\text{th}}
type-
l
parent in the
(n-1)^{\text{th}}
generation waited to give birth to its
(k^{\prime})^{\text{th}}
type-
i
child (here, the children are not ordered by age).

As in Definition
E.4
,
we use

R^{(n);j}_{i}

to denote, in the cluster
\mathcal{C}^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
,
the count of type-
i
descendants in the
(n-1)^{\text{th}}
generation,
and use the sequence
0<T^{(n);j}_{i}(1)<T^{(n);j}_{i}(2)<\ldots
to denote the birth times
of type-
i
descendants in the
n^{\text{th}}
generation (provided that the cluster does have at least
n
generations and there are type-
i
individuals in the
n^{\text{th}}
generation).
In particular, note that each
T^{(n);j}_{i}(r)
admits the expression

\displaystyle T^{(n);j}_{i}(r)=X_{j_{1}\leftarrow j}^{(1,1,k^{\prime}_{1})}+X_{j_{2}\leftarrow j_{1}}^{(2,k_{2},k^{\prime}_{2})}+\ldots+X_{i\leftarrow j_{n-1}}^{(n,k_{n},k^{\prime}_{n})},

where, for the (random) indices, we have

j_{l}\in[d]
,
k_{l}\in\big[R^{(l-1);j}_{j_{l-1}}\big]
, and

k^{\prime}_{l}\in\big[B_{j_{l}\leftarrow j_{l-1}}^{(n,k_{l-1});j}\big]

for each
l
;
here, note that we must have
j_{0}\equiv j
and
k_{1}\equiv 1
, since the only individual in the
0^{\text{th}}
generation is the type-
j
ancestor itself.
Next, note that

\displaystyle T^{(n);j}_{i}(r)

\displaystyle\leq X_{j_{1}\leftarrow j}^{(1,1,k^{\prime}_{1})}+X_{j_{2}\leftarrow j_{1}}^{(2,k_{2},k^{\prime}_{2})}+\ldots+X_{j_{n-1}\leftarrow j_{n-2}}^{(n-1,k_{n-1},k^{\prime}_{n-1})}+\max_{p,q\in[d]}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[R^{(n-1);j}_{p}]\\
k^{\prime}\in[B_{q\leftarrow p}^{(n,k);j}]\end{subarray}}X_{q\leftarrow p}^{(n,k,k^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}X_{j_{1}\leftarrow j}^{(1,1,k^{\prime}_{1})}+X_{j_{2}\leftarrow j_{1}}^{(2,k_{2},k^{\prime}_{2})}+\ldots+X_{j_{n-1}\leftarrow j_{n-2}}^{(n-1,k_{n-1},k^{\prime}_{n-1})}+\max_{p,q\in[d]}\max_{m\in[R^{(n);j}_{q}]}X_{q\leftarrow p}^{(m)}

\displaystyle\leq X_{j_{1}\leftarrow j}^{(1,1,k^{\prime}_{1})}+X_{j_{2}\leftarrow j_{1}}^{(2,k_{2},k^{\prime}_{2})}+\ldots+X_{j_{n-1}\leftarrow j_{n-2}}^{(n-1,k_{n-1},k^{\prime}_{n-1})}+\max_{p,q\in[d]}\max_{m\leq\sum_{l\in[d]}R^{(n);j}_{l}}X_{q\leftarrow p}^{(m)}.

In the display above,
the stochastic comparison (i.e., the
\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}
step)
holds due to the independence between the
X^{(n,k,k^{\prime})}_{q\leftarrow p}
’s and
\big(X^{(t,k,k^{\prime})}_{u\leftarrow v}\big)_{u,v\in[d],k\geq 1,k^{\prime}\geq 1,t\in[n-1]}
,
where we use
X^{(m)}_{q\leftarrow p}
to denote generic copies of
X_{q\leftarrow p}
(see (
E.51
)) that are independent of the
X^{(t,k,k^{\prime})}_{u\leftarrow v}
’s.
We emphasize that this stochastic comparison holds due to the
independence between the size (i.e., offspring counts) and the birth time distributions
in the cluster.
Repeating this argument inductively, we obtain

\displaystyle T^{(n);j}_{i}(r)

\displaystyle\underset{\text{s.t.}}{\leq}n\cdot\max_{p,q\in[d]}\ \max_{1\leq m\leq\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}\right\rVert}X_{q\leftarrow p}^{(m)}.

(E.52)

We note that (
E.52
) refines the bounds for the lifetime of clusters in
[
61
]
.

Adopting the notations in Definition
E.4
,
we let
K^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}=\max\{n\geq 0:\ R^{(n);j}_{i}\geq 1\text{ for some }i\in[d]\}

be the total count of generations in this cluster.
Besides, we use
R^{(n);j}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{i\in[d]}R^{(n);j}_{i}
to denote the offspring count in the
n^{\text{th}}
generation of the cluster process
C^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}(\cdot)
.
By (
E.52
),
for any
c_{1}>0
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(H_{j}>n\epsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}(K^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\geq c_{1}\log n)+\mathbf{P}\bigg(\max_{p,q\in[d]}\ \max_{1\leq m\leq\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}\right\rVert}X_{p\leftarrow q}^{(m)}>\frac{n\epsilon}{c_{1}\log n}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}(K^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\geq c_{1}\log n)+\sum_{p,q\in[d]}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\max_{1\leq m\leq\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}\right\rVert}X_{p\leftarrow q}^{(m)}>\frac{n\epsilon}{c_{1}\log n}\bigg).

To prove (
E.50
), it suffices to find some constant
c_{1}>0
(depending only on
\beta
and
j
) such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(K^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\geq c_{1}\log n)=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big)\ \text{ as }n\to\infty,

(E.53)

and that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\max_{1\leq m\leq\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}\right\rVert}X^{(m)}_{p\leftarrow q}>\frac{n\epsilon}{c_{1}\log n}\bigg)=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big)\ \text{ as }n\to\infty,\qquad\forall(p,q)\in[d]^{2}.

(E.54)

Proof of Claim (
E.53
)
.
We fix some
\tilde{\alpha}\in(1,\min_{p,q}\alpha_{p\leftarrow q})
.
By Assumption
2
,
we have

\mathbf{E}[\tilde{B}_{p\leftarrow q}^{\tilde{\alpha}}]<\infty.

Under this condition and
Assumption
1
,
it has been established in Section 3 of

[
51
]
(in particular, see Equation (6) in the paper and the discussion below)
that

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\big[(R^{(k);j})^{\tilde{\alpha}}\big]\leq c_{0}v^{k},\qquad\forall k\geq 1,

for some
c_{0}\in(0,\infty)
and
v\in(0,1)
whose values only depend on the law of the
\tilde{B}_{p,q}
’s.
Then, by Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(K^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\geq k)=\mathbf{P}\big(R^{(k);j}>0\big)=\mathbf{P}\big(R^{(k);j}\geq 1\big)\leq c_{0}v^{k},\qquad\forall k\geq 1.

By picking
c_{1}>0
large enough such that
c_{1}\log(v)<-\beta
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(K^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}\geq c_{1}\log n)\leq c_{0}\cdot\nu^{{c_{1}\log(n)}}=c_{0}\cdot n^{{c_{1}\log(\nu)}}=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big)

due to
a(n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\beta}(n)
.
This verifies Claim (
E.53
).

Proof of Claim (
E.54
)
.
Since the
X^{(m)}_{p\leftarrow q}
’s are independent of the
X_{p\leftarrow q}^{(n,k,k^{\prime})}
and
R_{p}^{(n);j}
(and hence the cluster
\mathcal{C}^{\mathcal{O}}_{j}
and the cluster size vector
\bm{S}_{j}
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\max_{1\leq m\leq\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}\right\rVert}X^{(m)}_{p\leftarrow q}>\frac{n\epsilon}{c_{1}\log n}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\max_{m\in[k]}X^{(m)}_{p\leftarrow q}>\frac{n\epsilon}{c_{1}\log n}\bigg)\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}\right\rVert=k)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}k\cdot\mathbf{P}\bigg(X_{p\leftarrow q}>\frac{n\epsilon}{c_{1}\log n}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}\right\rVert=k)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{E}\big[\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}\right\rVert\big]\cdot\mathbf{P}\bigg(X_{p\leftarrow q}>\frac{n\epsilon}{c_{1}\log n}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{E}\left\lVert\bm{S}_{j}\right\rVert}{\int_{0}^{\infty}f^{\bm{N}}_{p\leftarrow q}(t)dt}\cdot\int_{t>{n\epsilon}/({c_{1}\log n})}f^{\bm{N}}_{p\leftarrow q}(t)dt\qquad\text{by \eqref{proof, def law of X p q, lemma: lifetime of a cluster, LD for Hawkes}}

\displaystyle=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big)\qquad\text{due to \eqref{tail condition for fertility function, lemma: lifetime of a cluster, LD for Hawkes}}.

This concludes the proof of Claim (
E.54
).
∎

We prepare a lemma studying the distance between step functions under the metric
\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,T]}}}
in (
3.22
).

Lemma E.8
.

Let
T\in(0,\infty)
,
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\epsilon>0
, and
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{D}[0,T]=\mathbb{D}\big([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d}\big)
.
Suppose that

•

\xi
is a non-decreasing step function with
k
jumps and vanishes at the origin; i.e.,
\xi(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bm{w}_{i}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{i},T]}(t)\ \forall t\in[0,T]
,
where
\bm{w}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
for each
i\in[k]
and
0<t_{1}<\ldots<t_{k}=T
;

•

\xi^{\prime}
is a non-decreasing step function with
k^{\prime}
jumps and vanishes at the origin;

•

\xi\big((t-\epsilon)\vee 0\big)\leq\xi^{\prime}(t)\leq\xi(t)
for each
t\in[0,T]
.

Then, for any
t\in(0,T]
such that
t_{i}\notin[t-\epsilon,t]\ \forall i\in[k]
(i.e.,
\xi(\cdot)
remains constant over
[(t-\epsilon)\vee 0,t]
),

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,t]}}}(\xi,\xi^{\prime})\leq\epsilon.

Proof.

We write
\xi(t)=(\xi_{1}(t),\ldots,\xi_{d}(t)\big)^{\top}

and

\xi^{\prime}(t)=(\xi^{\prime}_{1}(t),\ldots,\xi^{\prime}_{d}(t)\big)^{\top}
.
Let
I=\{t\in(0,T]:\ t_{i}\notin[t,t+\epsilon]\ \forall i\in[k]\}
.
By definition of the product
M_{1}
metric in (
3.22
),
it suffices to show that
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,t]}}}(\xi_{i},\xi^{\prime}_{i})\leq\epsilon
for each
i\in[d]
and
t\in I
,
where
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,t]}}}
is the
M_{1}
metric for
\mathbb{R}
-valued càdlàg paths; see (
3.21
).
As a result, it suffices to prove the claim for
\mathbb{R}
-valued càdlàg paths.
More specifically,
let
T\in(0,\infty)
,
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
\epsilon>0
, and
x,y\in\mathbb{D}\big([0,T],\mathbb{R}\big)
, and suppose that

•

x
is a non-decreasing step function with
k
jumps and vanishes at the origin,

•

y
is a non-decreasing step function with
k^{\prime}
jumps and vanishes at the origin,

•

and
x\big((t-\epsilon)\vee 0\big)\leq y(t)\leq x(t)
for each
t\in[0,T]
.

It suffices to fix some
t\in(0,T]
such that
\Delta x(u)=0
for each
u\in[(t-\epsilon)\vee 0,t]
, and show that

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{M_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,t]}}}(x,y)\leq\epsilon.

(E.55)

Since both
x
and
y
are non-decreasing step functions over
[0,t]
, there exist
some
k^{*}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and a sequence

0=z_{0}<z_{1}<z_{2}<\ldots<z_{k^{*}}

such that

\displaystyle\big\{z\in[0,\infty):\ z=x(u)\text{ or }y(u)\text{ for some }u\in[0,t]\big\}=\{z_{0},z_{1},\ldots,z_{k^{*}}\}.

For each
l\in[k^{*}]
, let

\displaystyle t^{x}_{l}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\min\{u\geq 0:\ x(u)\geq z_{l}\},\quad t^{y}_{l}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\min\{u\geq 0:\ y(u)\geq z_{l}\}

be the first time
x(u)
(resp.,
y(u)
) crosses the level of
z_{l}
.
Due to
x\big((u-\epsilon)\vee 0\big)\leq y(u)\leq x(u)
for each
u\in[0,t]
,
we have

\displaystyle t^{y}_{l}-\epsilon\leq t^{x}_{l}\leq t^{y}_{l},\qquad\forall l\in[k^{*}].

(E.56)

Also, since
\Delta x(u)=0
for each
u\in[(t-\epsilon)\vee 0,t]
,
we know that the value of the step function
x(\cdot)
remains constant over
[(t-\epsilon)\vee 0,t]
.
Then by
x\big((t-\epsilon)\vee 0\big)\leq y(t)\leq x(t)
, at the right endpoint of the time interval
[0,t]
we have

\displaystyle x(t)=y(t)=z_{k^{*}}.

(E.57)

Now, we prove Claim (
E.55
) by constructing suitable parametric representations for paths
x
and
y
.
First, for the path
x
, we adopt the convention
t^{x}_{0}=0
, and define
\big(u^{x}(w),s^{x}(w)\big)
such that
(for each
l\in[k^{*}]
)
over the interval
w\in\big[\frac{2(l-1)}{2k^{*}+1},\frac{2l}{2k^{*}+1}\big)
,
the parametric representation
(u^{x},s^{x})
spends half of the time moving uniformly and horizontally from
(t^{x}_{l-1},z_{l-1})
to
(t^{x}_{l},z_{l-1})
over the connected graph of
x
,
and then spends the other half time moving uniformly and vertically from

(t^{x}_{l},z_{l-1})
to
(t^{x}_{l},z_{l})
.
More precisely, for each
l\in[k^{*}]
, let

\displaystyle u^{x}(w)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\begin{cases}t^{x}_{l-1}+(t^{x}_{l}-t^{x}_{l-1})\cdot\Big[(2k^{*}+1)w-{2(l-1)}\Big]&\text{if }w\in\big[\frac{2(l-1)}{2k^{*}+1},\frac{2(l-1)+1}{2k^{*}+1}\big)\\
t^{x}_{l}&\text{if }w\in\big[\frac{2(l-1)+1}{2k^{*}+1},\frac{2l}{2k^{*}+1}\big)\end{cases},

\displaystyle s^{x}(w)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\begin{cases}x\big(u^{x}(w)\big)=z_{l-1}&\text{if }w\in\big[\frac{2(l-1)}{2k^{*}+1},\frac{2(l-1)+1}{2k^{*}+1}\big)\\
z_{l-1}+(z_{l}-z_{l-1})\cdot\Big[(2k^{*}+1)w-\big(2(l-1)+1\big)\Big]&\text{if }w\in\big[\frac{2(l-1)+1}{2k^{*}+1},\frac{2l}{2k^{*}+1}\big)\end{cases}.

By definition,
x(u)
remains constant over
u\in[t_{k^{*}}^{x},t]
, so we set

\displaystyle u^{x}(w)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}t^{x}_{k^{*}}+(t-t^{x}_{k^{*}})\cdot\Big[(2k^{*}+1)w-{2k^{*}}\Big],\ \ s^{x}(w)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}z_{k^{*}},\qquad\forall w\in\bigg[\frac{2k^{*}}{2k^{*}+1},1\bigg].

Here, the choice of
s^{x}(w)=z_{k^{*}}
is valid due to the property (
E.57
).
Analogously, for the path
y
we define (for each
l\in[k^{*}]
)

\displaystyle u^{y}(w)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\begin{cases}t^{y}_{l-1}+(t^{y}_{l}-t^{y}_{l-1})\cdot\Big[(2k^{*}+1)w-{2(l-1)}\Big]&\text{if }w\in\big[\frac{2(l-1)}{2k^{*}+1},\frac{2(l-1)+1}{2k^{*}+1}\big)\\
t^{y}_{l}&\text{if }w\in\big[\frac{2(l-1)+1}{2k^{*}+1},\frac{2l}{2k^{*}+1}\big)\end{cases},

\displaystyle s^{y}(w)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\begin{cases}y\big(u^{y}(w)\big)=z_{l-1}&\text{if }w\in\big[\frac{2(l-1)}{2k^{*}+1},\frac{2(l-1)+1}{2k^{*}+1}\big)\\
z_{l-1}+(z_{l}-z_{l-1})\cdot\Big[(2k^{*}+1)w-\big(2(l-1)+1\big)\Big]&\text{if }w\in\big[\frac{2(l-1)+1}{2k^{*}+1},\frac{2l}{2k^{*}+1}\big)\end{cases},

and due to
y(u)\equiv z_{k^{*}}
for any
u\in[t^{y}_{k^{*}},t]
(see
(
E.57
)),
we set

\displaystyle u^{y}(w)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}t^{y}_{k^{*}}+(t-t^{y}_{k^{*}})\cdot\Big[(2k^{*}+1)w-{2k^{*}}\Big],\ \ s^{y}(w)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}z_{k^{*}},\qquad\forall w\in\bigg[\frac{2k^{*}}{2k^{*}+1},1\bigg].

Lastly, by our construction of the parametric representations
(u^{x},s^{x})
and
(u^{y},s^{y})
, we have
s^{y}(w)=s^{x}(w)
for any
w\in[0,1]
,
and
\sup_{w\in[0,1]}|u^{x}(w)-u^{y}(w)|\leq\epsilon
due to (
E.56
).
This concludes the proof of Claim (
E.55
).
∎

Equipped with Lemmas
E.7
and
E.8
,
we provide high probability bounds over the distance between
\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}
in (
E.34
) and
\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}
in (
E.42
)
under
\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}
.

Lemma E.9
.

Let Assumptions
1
–
4
hold.
Let
\beta>1

and
a:(0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
be a regularly varying function with
a(x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\beta}(x)
as
x\to\infty
.
Suppose that

\displaystyle\int_{x/\log x}^{\infty}f^{\bm{N}}_{p\leftarrow q}(t)dt=\mathit{o}\big(a(x)/x\big)\ \text{ as }x\to\infty,\qquad\forall p,q\in[d].

(E.58)

Then, for each
\Delta>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\big(a(n)\big)^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{P}\bigg(\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\Big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n},\ \bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}\Big)>\Delta\bigg)=0,\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough.}

Proof.

Analogous to the proof of Lemma
E.5
,
we fix some
T
large enough such that
e^{-T}<\Delta/2
,
and note that it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}e^{-t}\cdot\bigg[\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,t]}}}\Big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,t]}}_{n},\ \bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,t]}}_{n}\Big)\wedge 1\bigg]dt>\frac{\Delta}{2}\Bigg)=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big),\ \ \text{$\forall\delta>0$ small enough.}

(E.59)

On the one hand, by comparing the definitions in (
E.33
) and (
E.41
), we must have

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)\leq\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t),\qquad\forall\delta>0,\ n\geq 1,\ t\geq 0.

(E.60)

On the other hand,
recall that
for some cluster induced by a type-
j
immigrant,
we use
\bm{S}^{(k)}_{j}
to denote the cluster size,

T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}
for the arrival time of the immigrant inducing the cluster,
and

H^{(k)}_{j}
for the lifetime of the cluster (see (
E.43
)).
On the event

\big\{H^{(k)}_{j}\leq n\epsilon\ \forall(k,j)\text{ with }T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}\leq nT\big\},

we must have

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)\geq\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\big((t-\epsilon)\vee 0\big),\quad\forall\delta>0,\ n\geq 1,\ t\in[0,T].

(E.61)

Indeed, for any cluster induced by an immigrant arriving at time
T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}\leq n(t-\epsilon)
and with cluster lifetime
H^{(k)}_{j}\leq n\epsilon
,
all descendants in this cluster must have arrived by the time
nt
.
Furthermore, let
\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k)
be defined as in (
E.36
),
and note that the sequence of stopping times
\big(\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(k)\big)_{k\geq 1}
corresponds to the arrival times of jumps in
\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)
.
Therefore,
for any
K\geq 1
, on the event

A_{n}(K,T,\epsilon,\delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\{\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(K+1)>T\}\cap\big\{H^{(k)}_{j}\leq n\epsilon\ \forall(k,j)\text{ with }T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}\leq nT\big\},

we have

\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\big((t-\epsilon)\vee 0\big)\leq\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)\leq\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)

for any
t\in[0,T]
, and

\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,T]}}_{n}

is a step function that vanishes at the origin and has at most
K
jumps (all belonging to
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
).
By Lemma
E.8
,
there exists a Borel set
I\subseteq[0,T]
,
whose choice is random and depends on the paths
\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,t]}}_{n},\ \bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,t]}}_{n}
,
such that

•

\mathcal{L}_{\infty}(I)\leq K\epsilon
, where
\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
is the Lebesgue measure on
(0,\infty)
;

•

\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,t]}}}\big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,t]}}_{n},\ \bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,t]}}_{n}\big)\leq\epsilon

for any
t\in[0,T]\setminus I
.

Therefore, on the event
A_{n}(K,T,\epsilon,\delta)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}e^{-t}\cdot\bigg[\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,t]}}}\Big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,t]}}_{n},\ \bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,t]}}_{n}\Big)\wedge 1\bigg]dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t\in[0,T]\setminus I}\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,t]}}}\Big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta;_{[0,t]}}_{n},\ \bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta;_{[0,t]}}_{n}\Big)dt+\int_{t\in I}dt\leq T\epsilon+K\epsilon.

Furthermore, by picking
\epsilon>0
small enough we have
(T+K)\epsilon<\Delta/2
.
In summary, to prove Claim (
E.59
),
it suffices to show the existence of some
K\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\tau^{>\delta}_{n}(K+1)\leq T\Big)=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big),\qquad\text{$\forall\delta>0$ small enough},

(E.62)

and that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(H^{(k)}_{j}>n\epsilon\text{ for some }(k,j)\text{ with }T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}\leq nT\Big)=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big).

(E.63)

Proof of Claim (
E.62
)
.
This has been established in the proof of Claim (
E.37
) in Lemma
E.5
.

Proof of Claim (
E.63
)
.
It suffices to fix some
j\in[d]
,
\epsilon>0
and show that

\mathbf{P}\Big(H^{(k)}_{j}>n\epsilon\text{ for some }k\geq 1\text{ such that }T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k}\leq nT\Big)=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big).

Recall that
(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;k})_{k\geq 1}
are generated by a Poisson process with constant rate
c^{\bm{N}}_{j}
.
Therefore, by picking
C
large enough (whose value only depends on
T
and the constants
c^{\bm{N}}_{j}
),
it follows from Cramer’s Theorem that

\mathbf{P}\big(T^{\mathcal{C}}_{j;\lfloor nC\rfloor}\leq nT\big)=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big).

Fixing such
C
, we only need to show that

\mathbf{P}\big(H^{(k)}_{j}>n\epsilon\text{ for some }k\leq\lfloor nC\rfloor\big)=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big)

holds
for any
\delta>0
small enough.
However, since
H^{(k)}_{j}
are i.i.d. copies of
H_{j}
(see (
E.43
) and (
E.44
)),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(H^{(k)}_{j}>n\epsilon\text{ for some }k\leq\lfloor nC\rfloor\big)

\displaystyle\leq nC\cdot\mathbf{P}(H_{j}>n\epsilon)=nC\cdot\mathit{o}\big(a(n)/n\big)=\mathit{o}\big(a(n)\big).

The second to last equality follows from the condition (
E.58
) and Lemma
E.7
.
This concludes the proof of Claim (
E.63
).
∎

Lastly, we prove Proposition
3.5
.

Proof of Proposition
3.5
.

Due to

\displaystyle\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(\bar{\bm{N}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{n},\bar{\bm{L}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(\bar{\bm{N}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{n},\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta,_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}+\mu_{\bm{N}}\mathbf{1}\big)+\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta,_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}+\mu_{\bm{N}}\mathbf{1},\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta,_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}+\mu_{\bm{N}}\mathbf{1}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{n},\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta,_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}+\mu_{\bm{N}}\mathbf{1}\big)

\displaystyle=\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(\bar{\bm{N}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{n},\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta,_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}+\mu_{\bm{N}}\mathbf{1}\big)+\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta,_{[0,\infty)}}_{n},\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta,_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}\big)+\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,\infty)}}}\big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{{}_{[0,\infty)}}_{n},\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta,_{[0,\infty)}}_{n}+\mu_{\bm{N}}\mathbf{1}\big),

Proposition
3.5

follows directly from Lemmas
E.5
,
E.6
, and
E.9
.
∎

E.4
Univariate Results for
M_{1}
Topology of
\mathbb{D}[0,T]

We show that in the univariate setting,
Theorem
3.3
can be uplifted to
the
M_{1}
topology of
\mathbb{D}[0,T]
—the càdlàg space with compact domain.
Without loss of generality, throughout this section we fix
T=1
and adopt the notations

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{D}\big([0,1],\mathbb{R}\big),\quad\bm{d}_{M_{1}}=\bm{d}_{M_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,1]}}}.

We start by adapting the notions from the main paper to the univariate setting, as many of them now admit simpler or more explicit expressions.
First, the univariate Hawkes process
N(t)
has initial value
N(0)=0
and conditional intensity

\displaystyle h^{N}(t)=c_{N}+\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{B}(s)f^{N}(t-s)dN(s).

Here, the constant
c_{N}>0
is the arrival rate of immigrants,
the decay function
f^{N}(\cdot)
is such that
\left\lVert f^{N}\right\rVert_{1}<\infty
,
and the excitation rates
\big(\tilde{B}(s)\big)_{s>0}
are independent copies of
\tilde{B}

with
\mathbf{P}(\tilde{B}>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}(x)
for some
\alpha>1
.
Second, we adapt the tail asymptotics characterized in Section
2.2
for Hawkes processes clusters to the unvariate setting.
Let
S
be a generic copy for the size of a cluster.
For any
x>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(S>nx)\sim\frac{(\mathbf{E}S)^{1+\alpha}}{x^{\alpha}}\cdot\mathbf{P}(B>n),\quad\text{ as }n\to\infty,

(E.64)

where
B\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-3.01042pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}$}}\hskip-3.01042pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\text{Poisson}\big(\tilde{B}\cdot\left\lVert f^{N}\right\rVert_{1}\big)
.
Also, note that the regularly varying law of
\tilde{B}
implies

\mathbf{P}(B>x)\sim\mathbf{P}(\tilde{B}\cdot\left\lVert f^{N}\right\rVert_{1}>x)\sim\left\lVert f^{N}\right\rVert^{\alpha}_{1}\mathbf{P}(\tilde{B}>x).

The asymptotics (
E.64
)
are well-documented in existing literature on branching processes (e.g., Theorem 1 of
[
2
]
)
and are an immediate consequence of Theorem
2.2
in univariate settings.
In particular, using notations in Section
2.2
,
we have

\mathbf{P}(S>nx)\sim\lambda(n)\cdot\mathbf{C}_{S}\big((x,\infty)\big)

as
n\to\infty
,
where

\displaystyle\lambda(n)=\mathbf{P}(B>n);\qquad\mathbf{C}_{S}\big((x,\infty)\big)={(\mathbf{E}S)^{1+\alpha}}\big/{x^{\alpha}}\ \forall x>0.

(E.65)

Next, we adapt the notions introduced in Section
3

to the univariate setting.
By (
3.12
), for each
k\geq 1
we set

\displaystyle\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)=\big(n\mathbf{P}(B>n)\big)^{k}.

(E.66)

Analogous to (
3.7
),
for each
k\geq 0
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we define

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k;x}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Bigg\{x\bm{1}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{i},1]}:\ w_{i}\geq 0\text{ and }t_{i}\in(0,1]\ \forall i\in[k]\Bigg\},

where
\bm{1}(t)=t
is the identity mapping of
\mathbb{R}^{1}
.
That is,
\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k;x}
is the subset of
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{D}\big([0,1],\mathbb{R}\big)
containing any piece-wise linear function with slope
x
that vanishes at the origin and has at most
k
jumps (all upward).
Adapting (
3.25
) and (
3.27
), we define

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}=c_{N}\cdot\mathbf{C}_{S},\qquad\mu_{N}=c_{N}\cdot\mathbf{E}S.

(E.67)

We also adapt (
3.10
) and define Borel measures on
\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k;x}
by (for each
k\geq 1
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
)

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{k;x}(\ \cdot\ )

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k!}\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\int_{w_{i}>0\ \forall i\in[k]}\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{x\bm{1}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\mathbbm{I}_{[U_{i},1]}\in\ \cdot\ \bigg\}\bigtimes_{i=1}^{k}\mathbf{C}(dw_{i})\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{(\alpha c_{N})^{k}(\mathbf{E}S)^{k(1+\alpha)}}{k!}\mathbf{E}\Bigg[\int_{w_{i}>0\ \forall i\in[k]}\mathbbm{I}\bigg\{x\bm{1}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\mathbbm{I}_{[U_{i},1]}\in\ \cdot\ \bigg\}\bigtimes_{i=1}^{k}\frac{dw_{i}}{(w_{i})^{1+\alpha}}\Bigg],

where the sequence
(U_{i})_{i\geq 1}
are independent copies of Unif
(0,1)
, and the last line of the display above follows from the definition of
\mathbf{C}_{S}(\cdot)
in (
E.65
).
Now, let

\displaystyle\bar{N}_{n}(t)=N(nt)/n,\qquad\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}=\{\bar{N}_{n}(t):\ t\in[0,1]\}.

We are ready to state the strengthened univariate results.

Theorem E.10
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
A
be a Borel set of
\mathbb{D}
equipped with the Skorokhod
M_{1}
topology.
Suppose that

\displaystyle\int_{x/\log x}^{\infty}f^{\bm{N}}(t)dt=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{k}(x)\big/x\big),

(E.68)

and the set
A
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k-1;\mu_{N}}
under
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{k;\mu_{N}}(A^{\circ})\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}(\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}\in A)}{\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}(\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}\in A)}{\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)}\leq\breve{\mathbf{C}}_{k;\mu_{N}}(A^{-}),

where the interior and exterior sets
A^{\circ}
and
A^{-}
are determined under
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}

(i.e., w.r.t. the
M_{1}
topology of
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{D}\big([0,1],\mathbb{R}\big)
).

Since Theorem
E.10
is established w.r.t. the
M_{1}
topology of
\mathbb{D}[0,T]
(i.e., the càdlàg space with compact domain) instead of
\mathbb{D}[0,\infty)
,
our results now address a broader class of events or functional of
\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}

compared to only applying Theorem
3.3
directly to univariate Hawkes processes.
We conclude this subsection with the proof of Theorem
E.10
.

Proof of Theorem
E.10
.

The proof is in the same spirit as Theorem
3.3
.
In particular, like in (
3.32
) and (
3.35
),
we set
L(t)
as the compound Poisson process with
c_{N}
as arrival rates and
S
(the cluster size) as increments,
and let
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}=\{L(nt)/n:\ t\in[0,1]\}
be the scaled version of the sample path of
L(t)
embedded in
\mathbb{D}
.
By Proposition
3.4
and Lemma
A.3
,
it suffices to fix some positive integer
k
and verify the following:
Let
A
be a Borel set of
\mathbb{D}
equipped with the
M_{1}
topology such that
A
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k-1;\mu_{N}}
under
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}
;
for each
\Delta>0
we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{d}_{M_{1}}\big(\bar{\bm{N}}_{n},\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}\big)\mathbbm{I}\big\{\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}\text{ or }\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}\in A\big\}>\Delta\Big)=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)\big),\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(E.69)

To this end, we consider the definitions in (
E.32
)–(
E.34
) and (
E.40
)–(
E.42
) in the unvariate setting.
That is, we use
\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}
(resp.,
\bar{\bm{N}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}
) to denote a modified version of
\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}
where we only include clusters in
N(t)
whose sizes are larger than (resp., bounded by)
n\delta
.
We adopt similar notations
\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}
and
\bar{\bm{L}}^{\leqslant\delta}_{n}
for
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}
.
First, the proof of Claim (
E.35
) in Lemma
E.5
implies that for any
\Delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\bigg|\bar{L}_{n}(t)-\Big(\bar{L}_{n}^{>\delta}(t)+\mu_{\bm{N}}t\Big)\bigg|>\Delta\bigg)=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)\big),\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough.}

(E.70)

Similarly, the proof of Claim (
E.45
) in Lemma
E.6
implies that for any
\Delta>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\bigg|\bar{N}_{n}(t)-\Big(\bar{N}_{n}^{>\delta}(t)+\mu_{\bm{N}}t\Big)\bigg|>\Delta\bigg)=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)\big),\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough.}

(E.71)

Meanwhile, in (
E.69
) note that:
(i)
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}
is upper bounded by the uniform metric;
(ii)
whenever
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}\in A
and
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}\big(\bar{\bm{L}}_{n},\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\big)\leq\Delta
,
we must have
\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in A^{\Delta}
; the same can be argued for
\bar{\bm{N}}_{n}
;
and
(iii) for any
\Delta>0
small enough the set
A^{\Delta}
is still bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k-1;\mu_{N}}
.
Therefore, due to (
E.70
)–(
E.71
) and the arbitrariness of the choice of
A
and
\Delta>0
in (
E.69
),
it suffices to show the following:
Let
A
be a Borel set of
(\mathbb{D},\bm{d}_{M_{1}})
that is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k-1;\mu_{N}}
under
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}
and let
\Delta>0
;
for any
\delta>0
small enough we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{d}_{M_{1}}\big(\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n},\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\big)\mathbbm{I}\underbrace{\big\{\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in A\text{ or }\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in A\big\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-2.28471pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-2.28471pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}A_{n}(\delta)}>\Delta\Big)=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)\big).

(E.72)

Without loss of generality, in (
E.72
) we fix
\delta>0
, and take some
\Delta>0
small enough such that
A^{\Delta}
is still bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k-1;\mu_{N}}
under
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}
.
To proceed, we define a few events.
Let
H
be the lifetime of a generic copy of the cluster
S
, and the sequence
(S_{j},H_{j})
be independent copies of
(S,H)
.
Generate the sequence
0<T_{1}<T_{2}<\ldots
by a Poisson process with rate
c_{N}
, which denote the arrival times of each immigrant in
N(t)
.
By definition, the associated compound Poisson process admits the form
L(t)=\sum_{j\geq 1}S_{j}\mathbbm{I}\{T_{j}\leq t\}
.
For each
\epsilon>0
, define events

\displaystyle F_{n}(\epsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bigg\{H_{j}\leq n\epsilon\text{ for any }j\geq 1\text{ with }T_{j}\leq n\bigg\}.

That is, on the event
F_{n}(\epsilon)
, any immigrant that arrives by time
n
induces a cluster with lifetime shorter than
n\epsilon
.
Next, let
K^{>\delta}_{n}
be the count of jumps in
\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}
.
Due to the
\mathcal{O}(n)
time scaling in
\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}
,
we have
K^{>\delta}_{n}=\#\{j\geq 1:\ T_{j}\leq n,\ S_{j}>n\delta\}
.
Now, define events

\displaystyle E^{(1)}_{n}(\delta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big\{K^{>\delta}_{n}=k\Big\},\qquad E^{(2)}_{n}(\delta,\epsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big\{\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)-\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}(t-)=0\ \forall t\in[1-\epsilon,1]\Big\}.

Also, recall the definition of events
A_{n}(\delta)
in (
E.72
).
Suppose that (i) for any
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(F_{n}(\epsilon)\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle=o\big(\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)\big);

(E.73)

(ii) next,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Big(A_{n}(\delta)\cap F_{n}(\epsilon)\Big)\setminus E^{(1)}_{n}(\delta)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)\big),\quad\forall\epsilon\in(0,\Delta),

(E.74)

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Big(A_{n}(\delta)\cap F_{n}(\epsilon)\cap E^{(1)}_{n}(\delta)\Big)\setminus E^{(2)}_{n}(\delta,\epsilon)\bigg)\bigg/

\displaystyle\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)=0;

(E.75)

(iii) furthermore, for any
\epsilon\in(0,\Delta)
,

\displaystyle\text{it holds on the event }A_{n}(\delta)\cap F_{n}(\epsilon)\cap E^{(1)}_{n}(\delta)\cap E^{(2)}_{n}(\delta,\epsilon)

\displaystyle\text{ that }\bm{d}_{M_{1}}\big(\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n},\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}\big)\leq\Delta.

(E.76)

Then, the bound (
E.72
) follows directly from Claims (
E.73
)–(
E.76
).
Now, it only remains to verify these claims.

Proof of Claim (
E.73
)
.
This is an immediate consequence of Lemma
E.7
under condition (
E.68
).
See also
the proof of Claim (
E.63
) in Lemma
E.9
.

Proof of Claim (
E.74
)
.
By the law of the compound Poisson process
L(t)
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(K^{>\delta}_{n}\geq k+1\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(\text{Poisson}\big(n\mathbf{P}(S>n\delta)\big)\geq k+1\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\big(n\mathbf{P}(S>n\delta)\big)^{k+1}\qquad\text{due to }\mathbf{P}\big(\text{Poisson}(\lambda)\geq m\big)\leq\lambda^{m}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Big(\big(n\mathbf{P}(B>n)\big)^{k+1}\Big)=\mathit{o}\big(\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)\big)\qquad\text{by \eqref{univariate result, cluster tail asymptotics} and \eqref{def breve lambda k, univariate case}.}

Therefore, it suffices to show that for any
\epsilon\in(0,\Delta)
,

\displaystyle\Big\{\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in A\text{ or }\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in A\Big\}\cap F_{n}(\epsilon)\cap\Big\{K^{>\delta}_{n}\leq k-1\Big\}=\emptyset.

First, on
\big\{K^{>\delta}_{n}\leq k-1\big\}
, we have

\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k-1;\mu_{N}}.

Since
A
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k-1;\mu_{N}}
, this confirms that

\big\{\bar{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in A\big\}\cap\big\{K^{>\delta}_{n}\leq k-1\big\}=\emptyset.

Next, on the event
F_{n}(\epsilon)
, the lifetime of any cluster arriving by time
n
is upper bounded by
n\epsilon
.
Note that in
\bar{N}^{>\delta}_{n}
and
\bar{L}^{>\delta}_{n}
, due to the
\mathcal{O}(n)
time scaling,
this translates to an
\epsilon
upper bound time-wise.
Also, recall that we only consider
\epsilon\in(0,\Delta)
.
By the same arguments in Lemma
E.8
,
one can see that, on the event
F_{n}(\epsilon)\cap\big\{K^{>\delta}_{n}\leq k-1\big\}
,
by setting
\xi(t)=\bar{L}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)\wedge\bar{N}^{>\delta}_{n}(1)\ \forall t\in[0,1]
,
the path
\xi\in\mathbb{D}
admits the form

\displaystyle\xi(t)=\mu_{N}t+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}w_{i}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{i},1]}(t),\qquad\text{where }w_{i}\geq 0\text{ and }t_{i}\in(0,1]\ \forall i\in[k-1],

and satisfies
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}(\xi,\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n})<\epsilon<\Delta
.
This confirms that, on the event
F_{n}(\epsilon)\cap\big\{K^{>\delta}_{n}\leq k-1\big\}
, we must have

\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in(\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k-1;\mu_{N}})^{\Delta}.

However, this implies that on the event
F_{n}(\epsilon)\cap\big\{K^{>\delta}_{n}\leq k-1\big\}
, we must have

\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\notin A.

Indeed, note that

\displaystyle F_{n}(\epsilon)\cap\big\{K^{>\delta}_{n}\leq k-1\big\}\cap\big\{\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in A\big\}\subseteq\big\{\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in(\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k-1;\mu_{N}})^{\Delta}\cap A\big\}.

Meanwhile, recall the running assumption that
A^{\Delta}
is bounded away from
\breve{\mathbb{D}}_{k-1;\mu_{N}}
under
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}
,
which implies
(\mathbb{D}_{k-1;\mu_{N}})^{\Delta}\cap A=\emptyset
,
and hence

F_{n}(\epsilon)\cap\big\{K^{>\delta}_{n}\leq k-1\big\}\cap\big\{\bar{\bm{N}}^{>\delta}_{n}+\mu_{N}\mathbf{1}\in A\big\}=\emptyset.

This concludes the proof of Claim (
E.74
).

Proof of Claim (
E.75
)
.
It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\underbrace{K^{>\delta}_{n}=k;\ \exists t\in[1-\epsilon,1]\text{ such that }\bar{L}^{>\delta}_{n}(t)-\bar{L}^{>\delta}_{n}(t-)\neq 0}_{=E^{(1)}_{n}(\delta)\setminus E^{(2)}_{n}(\delta,\epsilon)}\bigg)\bigg/

\displaystyle\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)=0.

By the law of compound Poisson processes, we have (where the
U_{j}
’s are i.i.d. copies of Unif
(0,1)
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(E^{(1)}_{n}(\delta)\setminus E^{(2)}_{n}(\delta,\epsilon)\big)
