\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(\text{Poisson}\big(n\mathbf{P}(S>n\delta)\big)=k\Big)\cdot\mathbf{P}\Big(U_{j}\in[1-\epsilon,1]\text{ for some }j\in[k]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\big(n\mathbf{P}(S>n\delta)\big)^{k}\cdot k\epsilon

\displaystyle\sim k\epsilon\cdot\Bigg(\frac{(\mathbf{E}S)^{1+\alpha}}{\delta^{\alpha}}\Bigg)^{k}\cdot\breve{\lambda}_{k}(n)\qquad\text{by \eqref{univariate result, cluster tail asymptotics} and \eqref{def breve lambda k, univariate case}.}

Sending
\epsilon\downarrow 0
, we conclude the proof of Claim (
E.75
).

Proof of Claim (
E.76
)
.
This follows form Lemma
E.8
and our choice of
\epsilon\in(0,\Delta)
.
In particular, note that in the one-dimensional case, the product
M_{1}
metric
\bm{d}_{\mathcal{P}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,1]}}}
degenerates to the standard
M_{1}
metric
\bm{d}_{M_{1}}
on
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{D}\big([0,1],\mathbb{R}\big)
.
∎

Appendix F
Sample Path Large Deviations for Lévy Processes with
\mathcal{MHRV}
Increments (General Case)

In this section, we provide an analog of Theorem
3.2
—the sample path LDPs for Lévy Processes with
\mathcal{MHRV}
Increments—under relaxed conditions.
Such developments allow us to extend Theorem
3.2
beyond the context of Hawkes processes considered in the main paper and to cover a broader class of Lévy processes whose increments exhibit a more flexible form of multivariate hidden regular variation.

Specifically, we work with the following condition regarding the Lévy measure
\nu(\cdot)
of the
d
-dimensional Lévy process
\bm{L}(t)
.

Assumption 7

(
\mathcal{MHRV}
increments in Lévy Processes (General Case))
.

The Lévy measure
\nu(\cdot)
of
\bm{L}(t)
is supported on
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}

and satisfies

\nu\in\mathcal{MHRV}\Big((\bar{\bm{s}}_{j})_{j\in[m]},\big(\alpha(\bm{j})\big)_{\bm{j}\subseteq[m]},(\lambda_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}},(\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\Big)

under

•

some positive integer
m\in[d]
,

•

a collection of vectors
(\bar{\bm{s}}_{j})_{j\in[m]}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
that are linearly independent,

•

a collection of real numbers
(\alpha(\bm{j}))_{\bm{j}\subseteq[m]}
that are strictly monotone w.r.t.
\bm{j}
(see (
2.1
)) with
\alpha(\emptyset)=0
,

\alpha(\bm{j})>1\ \forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}
, and
\alpha(\bm{j})\neq\alpha(\bm{j}^{\prime})\ \forall\bm{j},\bm{j}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}
with
\bm{j}\neq\bm{j}^{\prime}
;

•

regularly varying functions
\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha(\bm{j})}(n)
(for each
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}
),

•

Borel measures
(\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\subseteq[m]}
where, for each
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}
,
the measure
\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}
is
supported on
\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j})
,
such that

\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}(A)<\infty

holds for any
\epsilon>0
and Borel set
A\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
bounded away from
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)
(see (
2.4
)).

Throughout this section, we denote the basis by
\bar{\textbf{S}}=\{\bar{\bm{s}}_{j}:\ j\in[m]\}
and the collection of tail indices by
\bm{\alpha}=\big(\alpha(\bm{j})\big)_{\bm{j}\subseteq[m]}
.
Besides, we adopt the simplified notations

{\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j})}={\mathbb{R}^{d}(\bm{j};\bar{\textbf{S}})},

{\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)}=\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon;\bar{\textbf{S}}),

and

{\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\epsilon)}={\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}_{\leqslant}(\bm{j},\epsilon;\bar{\textbf{S}},\bm{\alpha})}

in Section
2.1
.
Compared to Assumption
5
, the key differences in Assumption
7
are that

•

we drop the condition (
3.2
) on the rate functions
\lambda_{\bm{j}}(\cdot)
;

•

we now consider a weaker
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition (instead of the
\mathcal{MHRV}^{*}
condition in Assumption
5
); also, the number of base vectors
\bar{s}_{j}
considered is not fixed as
d
.

When imposing the more flexible Assumption
7
, one potential issue is that we remain agnostic
about
the tail behaviors outside of the cone
\mathbb{R}^{d}([m])
.
This potential lack of knowledge about the hidden regular variation in certain regions
is reflected in Theorem
F.1
—the main result of this section—through the following definition:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon;\bar{\textbf{S}})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\begin{cases}\bar{\mathbb{R}}(\bm{j},\epsilon;\bar{\textbf{S}})&\text{if }\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m},\ \bm{j}\neq[m]\\
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}&\text{if }\bm{j}=[m]\end{cases}.

(F.1)

In other words,
we only modify the definition
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon;\bar{\textbf{S}})
to include the entire domain
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}
.
We also write
\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon;\bar{\textbf{S}})
as there will be no ambiguity about
\bar{\textbf{S}}
in this section.

We introduce a few notions that are required for the statement of Theorem
F.1
.
Recall that
we use notations like
\bm{k}=(k_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{J}}\in A^{\mathcal{J}}

to denote vectors of length
|\mathcal{J}|
with each coordinate taking values in
A
and indexed by elements in
\mathcal{J}
.
Besides, recall that we use
{\mathcal{P}}_{m}
to denote the collection of all
non-empty
subsets of
[m]
.
Analogous to (
3.7
),
given
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
,
we define

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{x}}[0,T]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Bigg\{\bm{x}\bm{1}+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},T]}:

(F.2)

\displaystyle t_{\bm{j},k}\in(0,T]\text{ and }\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\in\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)\ \forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}];\ t_{\bm{j},k}\neq t_{\bm{j}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\ \forall(\bm{j},k)\neq(\bm{j}^{\prime},k^{\prime})\Bigg\},

i.e., the set of all paths
\xi
of the form

\displaystyle\xi(t)=\bm{x}t+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},T]}(t),\qquad\forall t\in[0,T],

(F.3)

where
each
\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}
belongs to the cone
\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)
,
and any two numbers in the sequence
(t_{\bm{j},k})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m},\ k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}
are not equal (i.e., the arrival times of jumps would not coincide).
Next, define

\displaystyle{\hat{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}\cdot\Big(\alpha(\bm{j})-1\Big),\qquad\forall\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}.

(F.4)

Note that this agrees with
\breve{c}(\cdot)
in (
3.9
),
and we repeat the definition here so that
{\hat{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})}
is consistent with other notations introduced in this section.
Given
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\epsilon\geq 0
,
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
,
let

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant{\bm{\mathcal{K}}};\bm{x}}[0,T]\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}:\\
\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}\neq\bm{\mathcal{K}},\ \hat{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime})\leq\hat{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})\end{subarray}}\hat{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime};\bm{x}}[0,T].

(F.5)

Meanwhile, given
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
, let

\displaystyle{\hat{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(n)}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\Big(n\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\Big)^{\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}},

(F.6)

where
\lambda_{\bm{j}}(n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha(\bm{j})}(n)
is the rate function of the
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition in Assumption
7
.
Then, by the definition of
\hat{c}(\cdot)
in (
F.4
), we have

\hat{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(n)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\hat{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})}(n).

Lastly,
analogous to (
3.10
),
given

\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
,
we define the Borel measure

\displaystyle{\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{x}}(\ \cdot\ )}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\frac{1}{\prod_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}!}

(F.7)

\displaystyle\cdot\int\mathbbm{I}\Bigg\{\bm{x}\bm{1}+\sum_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\sum_{k\in[\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}}]}\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k}\mathbbm{I}_{[t_{\bm{j},k},T]}\in\ \cdot\ \Bigg\}\bigtimes_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\bigtimes_{k\in[k_{\bm{j}}]}\bigg((\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}\times\mathcal{L}_{(0,T)})\Big(d(\bm{w}_{\bm{j},k},t_{\bm{j},k})\Big)\bigg),

where the
\mathbf{C}_{\bm{j}}
’s are the limiting measures for the
\mathcal{MHRV}
condition of Assumption
7
,

\mathcal{L}_{(0,T)}
is the Lebesgue measure on the interval
(0,T)
,
and we use
\nu_{1}\times\nu_{2}
to denote the product measure of
\nu_{1}
,
\nu_{2}
.

Let
\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}
and
\bar{\bm{L}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}_{n}
be defined as in (
3.4
)–(
3.5
).
We are now ready to state Theorem
F.1
.

Theorem F.1
.

Let Assumption
7
hold.
Let
T\in(0,\infty)
,

\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
,
and let
B
be a Borel set of
\mathbb{D}[0,T]
equipped with the Skorokhod
J_{1}
topology.
Suppose that
B
is bounded away from

\hat{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,T]

under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}
for some (and hence all)
\epsilon>0
small enough.
Then,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}(B^{\circ})\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}_{n}\in B\big)}{\hat{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(n)}\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{\mathbf{P}\big(\bar{\bm{L}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}_{n}\in B\big)}{\hat{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(n)}\leq\hat{\mathbf{C}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}(B^{-})<\infty.

In other words,
Theorem
F.1
addresses the general case where a more flexible (and hence less detailed) description of the multivariate hidden regular variation (as in Assumption
7
) for the Lévy measure
\nu(\cdot)
is available, at the cost of slightly weaker characterizations of sample path large deviations when compared to Theorem
3.2
.
In particular,
without conditions like (
3.2
),
we do not know if the rate functions
\hat{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(n)
are comparable (as
n\to\infty
) for different
\bm{\mathcal{K}}
if their tail indices
\hat{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})
are equal;
as a result,
Theorem
F.1

does not allow the merging of multiple cases corresponding to different
\bm{\mathcal{K}}
with equal tail indices
\hat{c}(\bm{\mathcal{K}})
.
Besides,
the modification from
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)
to
\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)
in (
F.1
)
enlarges
the set
\hat{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,T]

in (
F.2
)
(when compared to
\breve{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,T]
in (
3.7
)).
This generally leads to fewer sets
B
that are bounded away from
\hat{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}}[0,T]
,
and thus fewer combinations of rare events and the rates
\bm{\mathcal{K}}
to which Theorem
F.1
can be applied.

The proof of Theorem
F.1
is almost identical to that of Theorem
3.2
stated in Section
D
,
and we provide a proof sketch below for the sake of completeness.

Proof Sketch for Theorem
F.1
.

Analogous to Theorem
3.2
,
w.l.o.g. we consider the case where
T=1
and
\bm{\mu}_{\bm{L}}=\bm{0}
,
and adopt the notations
\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}=\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}^{{}_{[0,1]}}
,
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{D}\big([0,1],\mathbb{R}^{d}\big)
, and
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}=\bm{d}_{J_{1}}^{{}_{{}_{[0,1]}}}
.
Again, the proof relies on the notion of asymptotic equivalence for
\mathbb{M}
-convergence.
By Lemma
A.3
,
it suffices to verify the following two conditions:

1.

For each
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}

and
\Delta>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{d}_{J_{1}}\big(\hat{\bm{L}}_{n}^{>\delta},\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}\big)>\Delta\Big)\Big/\hat{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(n)=0,\qquad\forall\delta>0\text{ small enough,}

where
\hat{\bm{L}}_{n}^{>\delta}
is defined in (
D.2
)–(
D.4
).

2.

Let
\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
and
\epsilon>0
.
Let
f:\mathbb{D}\to[0,\infty)
be bounded and continuous (w.r.t. the
J_{1}
topology of
\mathbb{D}
).
Suppose
that

B=\text{supp}(f)

is bounded away from
\hat{\mathbb{D}}^{\epsilon}_{\leqslant\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{0}}[0,1]

under
\bm{d}_{J_{1}}
.
Then, there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that for all but countably many
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbf{E}\big[f(\hat{\bm{L}}^{>\delta}_{n})\big]}\big/{\hat{\lambda}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}}}(n)}=\hat{\mathbf{C}}^{{}_{[0,1]}}_{\bm{\mathcal{K}};\bm{0}}(f)<\infty.

One can see that these two conditions are similar to the statements in Propositions
D.2
and
D.3
.
Likewise, these two conditions can be verified by repeating the arguments in Section
D.2
.
The only key difference stems from the definition of the modified cones
\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\epsilon)
in (
F.1
).
In particular,
any vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}\setminus\mathbb{R}^{d}([m])
would now fall into
\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}([m],\epsilon)
(under any
\epsilon\geq 0
).
Therefore, when keeping track of the large jumps in
\bm{L}(t)
, there is no need to specifically count those that lie outside of
\mathbb{R}^{d}([m])
(i.e., the notions of
\tilde{\tau}^{>\delta}_{n}(k)
and
\widetilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}
introduced in (
D.12
)–(
D.13
) are not needed here).
Instead,
for each
\delta>0
,
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}
, let

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Bigg\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{+}:\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert>\delta,\ \bm{x}\in\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j},\delta)\setminus\bigg(\bigcup_{\bm{j}^{\prime}\subseteq[m]:\ \bm{j}^{\prime}\neq\bm{j},\ \alpha(\bm{j}^{\prime})\leq\alpha(\bm{j})}\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j}^{\prime},\delta)\bigg)\Bigg\},

which adapts the definition of
\bar{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})
in (
D.5
) to our current setting.
Next,
for each
\delta>0
,
\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}
, and
k\geq 1
, we define
(under the convention that
{\hat{\tau}^{>\delta}_{n}(0;\bm{j})}\equiv 0
)

\displaystyle{\hat{\tau}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\inf\Big\{t>\hat{\tau}^{>\delta}_{n}(k-1;\bm{j}):\ \Delta\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}(t)\in\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{>\delta}(\bm{j})\Big\},\qquad{\hat{\bm{W}}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Delta\bar{\bm{L}}_{n}\Big(\hat{\tau}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})\Big).

Besides, for each
\delta>0
, let

\displaystyle{\hat{K}^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{j})}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\max\{k\geq 0:\ \hat{\tau}^{>\delta}_{n}(k;\bm{j})\leq 1\},\quad\forall\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m};\qquad{\hat{\bm{K}}^{>\delta}_{n}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 4.49658pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-4.49658pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\text{def}$}}\hskip-4.49658pt\hskip 4.49658pt}}\Big({\hat{K}^{>\delta}_{n}(\bm{j})}\Big)_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}.

In short, we adapt the definitions in (
D.8
)–(
D.11
)
to count each type of large jumps in
\bm{L}(t)
based on their locations w.r.t. the (modified) cones
\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{d}(\bm{j})
in (
F.1
), which are generated by the basis described in Assumption
7
.
Then, to prove conditions (i) and (ii) listed above,
it only remains to repeat the proofs in Section
D
.
The changes are minimal and, in fact, the arguments are now even simpler.
For instance, when adapting Lemma
D.6
,
as noted above there is no need to consider
\tilde{K}^{>\delta}_{n}
anymore.
Besides, in Lemmas
D.7
and
D.8
,
the notion of allocation
\mathbb{A}(\bm{k})
is not needed
(as noted right after Theorem
F.1
, we do not consider merging the cases corresponding to different
\bm{\mathcal{K}}
here),
so it suffices to fix some

\bm{\mathcal{K}}=(\mathcal{K}_{\bm{j}})_{\bm{j}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{{\mathcal{P}}_{m}}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
in the proof.
The arguments are almost identical, and we omit the details here to avoid repetition.
∎
